### src/Analysis/Generic/Domain/Abstract_Domain.thy

> The solver unknown for a program point is a CFG node. Analysis-facing code keeps the short name `pp` for it; a return node is `FunctionResult p`, a callee entry `FunctionEntry p`, an ordinary location `Statement n`.

### src/Analysis/Generic/Domain/Exec_Backward.thy

> Every interpretation of `backward_domain` gets an executable `"'a st"` mirror of its *afilter* / *bfilter* for free: `afilter_st` / `bfilter_st` and their commutation with the abstract filters through `fun_of_st`, proved once here by the same induction every domain previously repeated by hand. A concrete domain names its own specialization via the `defines` clause of its existing `backward_domain` interpretation (see `Sign_Backward`, `Interval_Backward`); no per-domain proof is needed.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `afilter_st` | 21 | fun afilter_st :: "aexp ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a st ⇒ 'a st" where<br>    "afilter_st (V x) a s =<br>       update_st s x (meet a (lookup_st s x))"<br>  \| "afilter_st (Plus e1 e2) a s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_plus a (aval_abs e1 (lookup_st s)) (aval_abs e2 (lookup_st s))<br>        in afilter_st e1 a1 (afilter_st e2 a2 s))"<br>  \| "afilter_st (Minus e1 e2) a s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_minus a (aval_abs e1 (lookup_st s)) (aval_abs e2 (lookup_st s))<br>        in afilter_st e1 a1 (afilter_st e2 a2 s))"<br>  \| "afilter_st (Times e1 e2) a s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_times a (aval_abs e1 (lookup_st s)) (aval_abs e2 (lookup_st s))<br>        in afilter_st e1 a1 (afilter_st e2 a2 s))"<br>  \| "afilter_st _ a s = s" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Domain/Split_State.thy

> A split abstract state keeps the local and the global half of an abstract store in two separate components with independent value types: `'l abs_state` for locals and `'g abs_state` for globals. Each component is a total `vname => _` map; well-formedness (`wf_split`) pins the off-domain half of each component to `bot`. At the homogeneous instance `'l = 'g` the split representation is isomorphic to the plain state `'a abs_state`: `merge_state` and `split_state` are mutually inverse between well-formed split states and plain states (`merge_state_bij`), the order transports both ways (`merge_state_le_iff`), lattice operations commute with the conversions, and the split concretization `gamma_split` agrees with `gamma_state` under the isomorphism.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `split_state` | 23 | type_synonym ('l, 'g) split_state = "'l abs_state × 'g abs_state" | A split abstract state keeps the local and the global half of an abstract store in two separate components with independent value types: `'l abs_state` for locals and `'g abs_state` for globals. Each component is a total `vname => _` map; well-formedness (`wf_split`) pins the off-domain half of each component to `bot`. At the homogeneous instance `'l = 'g` the split representation is isomorphic to the plain state `'a abs_state`: `merge_state` and `split_state` are mutually inverse between well-formed split states and plain states (`merge_state_bij`), the order transports both ways (`merge_state_le_iff`), lattice operations commute with the conversions, and the split concretization `gamma_split` agrees with `gamma_state` under the isomorphism. |
| definition | `wf_split` | 27 | definition wf_split :: "('l::bot, 'g::bot) split_state ⇒ bool" where<br>  "wf_split lg ↔<br>     (∀x. is_global x ⟶ fst lg x = bot) ∧<br>     (∀x. ¬ is_global x ⟶ snd lg x = bot)" |  |
| definition | `merge_state` | 49 | definition merge_state :: "('a, 'a) split_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "merge_state lg = (λx. if is_global x then snd lg x else fst lg x)" |  |
| definition | `split_state` | 52 | definition split_state :: "('a::bot) abs_state ⇒ ('a, 'a) split_state" where<br>  "split_state σ =<br>     ((λx. if is_global x then bot else σ x),<br>      (λx. if is_global x then σ x else bot))" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Equations/CFG_Enumeration.thy

> Backward predecessor relations and deterministic edge enumeration over a compiled two-relation CFG. These are the equation-generation and code-generation views the TD solver reads to build and evaluate the constraint system; they are not part of the concrete CFG semantics --- `valid_ltr` never refers to them. Ordinary control flow is enumerated over `intra`; procedure calls --- entry routing and return combining --- over `calls`. There is no unified edge set and no separate combine relation: a return is recovered from the same `calls` tuple that created the activation.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `intra_predecessors` | 34 | definition intra_predecessors :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action) set" where<br>  "intra_predecessors g v = {(u, a). (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}" | The ordinary incoming transitions of a node: an intra edge is a total store transformer within one activation, so this is the transfer fold the equation system runs at every node --- including the `EA_Ret e p` edges into `FunctionResult p`, by which return values are summarised as ordinary predecessor folding. |
| definition | `entry_calls` | 58 | definition entry_calls :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action) set" where<br>  "entry_calls g v = {(c, ca). ∃k. (c, ca, v, k) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose callee entry is the queried node `v` (a `FunctionEntry p`). The continuation does not affect the callee-entry state, so it is projected away; the entry contribution is the caller state routed through the call action's parameter binding (`tf_enter`). |
| definition | `return_calls` | 84 | definition return_calls ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × vname option × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "return_calls g v =<br>     {(c, dst, FunctionResult p) \| c dst formals actuals p.<br>        (c, CallEdge dst formals actuals, FunctionEntry p, v) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose continuation is the queried node `v`, each paired with the caller destination and the callee's `FunctionResult p` exit node --- recovered from the callee entry `FunctionEntry p`, so no separate combine relation is needed. The return contribution is the caller state and the callee-exit state assembled by `combine_collect_abs`. The final component is returned directly so clients need not rebuild the result node. |
| definition | `cfg_intra_list` | 113 | definition cfg_intra_list :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "cfg_intra_list g =<br>     (if finite (intra g) then sorted_list_of_set (intra g) else [])" | Stable list views for the TD bridge: the intra and call edge sets sorted by their structural order, and the queried-node projections built by filtering. |
| definition | `cfg_calls_list` | 121 | definition cfg_calls_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "cfg_calls_list g =<br>     (if finite (calls g) then sorted_list_of_set (calls g) else [])" |  |
| definition | `intra_predecessor_list` | 138 | definition intra_predecessor_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action) list" where<br>  "intra_predecessor_list g v =<br>     map (λ(u, a, w). (u, a)) (filter (λ(u, a, w). w = v) (cfg_intra_list g))" |  |
| definition | `entry_call_list` | 149 | definition entry_call_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action) list" where<br>  "entry_call_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, ca)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). ce = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `return_call_list` | 159 | definition return_call_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × vname option × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "return_call_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, case ca of CallEdge dst _ _ ⇒ dst,<br>                              case ce of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| _ ⇒ ce))<br>       (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>          k = v ∧ (case ce of FunctionEntry _ ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `return_call_actions` | 188 | definition return_call_actions ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "return_call_actions g v =<br>     {(c, ca, FunctionResult p) \| c ca p. (c, ca, FunctionEntry p, v) ∈ calls g}" | `return_call_list` keeps only the caller destination projected from the triggering `call_action`, which is all the flat/context-insensitive combine (`combine_collect_abs`, via `Constraint_System.rhs_combine_sources`) needs. The context-sensitive DG generator needs more: it must route both the callee-entry seed publication and the return-side context read from the *same* call action, and `entry_call_list` already keeps `ca` whole on the entry side. This is the return-side counterpart that does the same --- kept as a separate enumeration rather than widening `return_call_list` itself, since that function and its `vname option` shape are also relied on by the flat constraint system and the homogeneous effectful-transfer solver core (`TD_Side_Tree.thy`), which are out of scope for context routing. |
| definition | `return_call_action_list` | 209 | definition return_call_action_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "return_call_action_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, ca,<br>                              case ce of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| _ ⇒ ce))<br>       (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>          k = v ∧ (case ce of FunctionEntry _ ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `call_successors` | 245 | definition call_successors ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "call_successors g v = {(ce, ca, k). (v, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose call site is the queried node `v`: the entry, action, and continuation of every call `v` makes. This is the caller-side counterpart of `entry_calls` (callee-indexed) and `return_call_actions` (continuation-indexed) --- routing a call-entry seed publication at its call site needs the callee entry and the whole `call_action` together, which neither of those two enumerations exposes. |
| definition | `call_successor_list` | 263 | definition call_successor_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "call_successor_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (ce, ca, k)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Equations/Constraint_System.thy

> Given: - A CFG g - An abstract domain D (instance of abstract_domain) - Per-domain transfer functions for each edge action We construct an equation system (constraint system) where each program point v has an equation: σ(v) = join_over { tf(a)(σ(u)) \| (u,a,v) in g } plus the special base case at the entry: σ(entry) includes the initial abstract state. The equation system is represented as an RHS function: rhs :: pp => (pp => D abs_state) => D abs_state This is the format expected by the top-down solver locale. All transfer functions are parameterised over the domain via a locale so the same equation-system construction works for Sign, Interval, etc.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| record | `domain_transfer` | 38 | record 'a domain_transfer =<br>  tf_assign    :: "vname =&gt; aexp =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_assume    :: "bexp  =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_assume_not :: "bexp =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_enter     :: "vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_combine   :: "('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)" | A domain_transfer bundles the per-action abstract transformers. Parameterised by the abstract value type 'a. |
| fun | `apply_tf` | 47 | fun apply_tf :: "'a domain_transfer<br>                 =&gt; edge_action<br>                 =&gt; ('a abs_state)<br>                 =&gt; ('a abs_state)" where<br>    "apply_tf tf EA_Nop              σ = σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Assign x a)     σ = tf_assign tf x a σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Assume b)       σ = tf_assume tf b σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_AssumeNot b)    σ = tf_assume_not tf b σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Ret e p)        σ =<br>       (case e of None ⇒ σ \| Some a ⇒ tf_assign tf ret_var a σ)" |  |
| definition | `abs_join_set` | 105 | definition abs_join_set ::<br>    "('a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state)<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state set<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "abs_join_set join_abs bot_abs S = Finite_Set.fold join_abs bot_abs S" | Fold join_abs over a finite set of abstract states. Requires comp_fun_commute join_abs for the result to be order-independent. Finiteness of the predecessor set follows from finite (intra g) and finite (calls g). |
| definition | `combine_abs` | 316 | definition combine_abs :: "'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" ("\&lt;langle&gt;_\|_\&lt;rangle&gt;") where<br>  "combine_abs sc se = (λx. if is_global x then se x else sc x)" |  |
| definition | `bind_formals_abs` | 342 | definition bind_formals_abs ::<br>    "vname list ⇒ 'a list ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "bind_formals_abs xs avs σ = fold (λ(x, a) \&lt;tau&gt;. \&lt;tau&gt;(x := a)) (zip xs avs) σ" | Formal binding evaluates actual arguments in the caller state and assigns each abstract value to the matching callee formal. |
| definition | `enter_frame_D` | 432 | definition enter_frame_D :: "'a ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "enter_frame_D top_val σ = (λx. if is_global x then σ x else top_val)" | Generic procedure-entry frame: reset locals to a fixed, fully-imprecise `top_val`, keep globals, then bind the formals via `bind_formals_abs`. Every domain's own enter-frame construction (Sign's *enter_frame_sign*, Interval's *enter_frame_ivl*) is this same shape, differing only in which value stands for "unknown" -- so this is parameterised over that one value rather than duplicated per domain. |
| definition | `enter_D` | 458 | definition enter_D ::<br>  "'a ⇒ (aexp ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a) ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "enter_D top_val aval_abs xs es σ =<br>     bind_formals_abs xs (map (λe. aval_abs e σ) es) (enter_frame_D top_val σ)" |  |
| fun | `combine_assign_abs` | 491 | fun combine_assign_abs ::<br>    "vname option ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>    "combine_assign_abs None _ σ = σ"<br>  \| "combine_assign_abs (Some x) v σ = σ(x := v)" | Abstract counterpart of `combine_assign`: write the callee's return slot into the caller's destination, or leave the caller untouched when the call discards its result. |
| definition | `combine_collect_abs` | 501 | definition combine_collect_abs ::<br>    "vname option ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "combine_collect_abs dst σc σe = combine_assign_abs dst (σe ret_var) \&lt;langle&gt;σc\|σe\&lt;rangle&gt;" | Return combination joins caller locals with callee globals and then assigns the callee's `ret_var` to the optional destination. The ordinary abstract state update publishes the result without domain-specific return machinery. |
| definition | `tf_combine_collect_abs` | 536 | definition tf_combine_collect_abs ::<br>    "'a domain_transfer ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "tf_combine_collect_abs tf dst σc σe = combine_assign_abs dst (σe ret_var) (tf_combine tf σc σe)" | Per-analysis return combine. `combine_collect_abs` fixes the environment merge to `combine_abs`; `tf_combine_collect_abs` instead reads the merge from the `'a domain_transfer` in scope, so each analysis may supply its own sound over-approximation of `combine_states` instead of the structural local/global split. The return-value write stays `combine_assign_abs`: it is already domain-agnostic under the function-based `'a abs_state` representation, so only the merge step is a per-analysis choice. |
| definition | `rhs_edge_sources` | 600 | definition rhs_edge_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_edge_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(u, a). apply_tf tf a (env u)) ` intra_predecessors g v" | Each predecessor kind contributes a set of abstract states. Keeping the families separate gives downstream proofs precise introduction rules; their union is the single source of truth used by the executable right-hand side. |
| definition | `rhs_entry_sources` | 606 | definition rhs_entry_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_entry_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(c, ca). case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ tf_enter tf fs as (env c))<br>       ` entry_calls g v" |  |
| definition | `rhs_combine_sources` | 613 | definition rhs_combine_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_combine_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(c, dst, ex). tf_combine_collect_abs tf dst (env c) (env ex)) ` return_calls g v" |  |
| definition | `rhs_sources` | 618 | definition rhs_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_sources g tf env v =<br>     rhs_edge_sources g tf env v ∪<br>     rhs_entry_sources g tf env v ∪<br>     rhs_combine_sources g tf env v" |  |
| definition | `rhs` | 660 | definition rhs ::<br>    "cfg<br>     ⇒ 'a domain_transfer<br>     ⇒ ('a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "rhs g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env v =<br>     abs_join_set join_abs bot_abs<br>       (if v = cfg_entry g then insert s0 (rhs_sources g tf env v)<br>        else rhs_sources g tf env v)" | `rhs` is the equation system's right-hand side at `v`: the join over `rhs_sources`'s abstract predecessor contributions, plus the seed `s0` exactly when `v` is the graph's entry --- the one node with no predecessor edge to supply a starting value otherwise. |
| definition | `is_post_fixpoint` | 675 | definition is_post_fixpoint ::<br>    "cfg<br>     ⇒ ('a::ord domain_transfer)<br>     ⇒ ('a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "is_post_fixpoint g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env =<br>     (∀v. rhs g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env v ≤ env v)" |  |
| definition | `cinit_stores` | 748 | definition cinit_stores :: "store set" where<br>  "cinit_stores = {s. ∀x. is_global x ⟶ s x = 0}" | In C, global variables are zero-initialised before `main` starts (ISO C 6.7.9p10); local variables are uninitialized and may hold any integer value. `cinit_stores` is the corresponding set of concrete stores: those where every global is 0 and locals are unconstrained. Any analysis that uses a domain-specific abstract seed `s0` satisfying `cinit_stores ⊆ gamma_state s0` may state its soundness theorem against `cinit_stores` rather than `UNIV`, matching C program semantics. |
| locale | `sound_transfer` | 758 | locale sound_transfer =<br>  fixes tf :: "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assign:<br>    "∀x (a::aexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧.<br>       s(x := aval a s) ∈ ⟦tf_assign tf x a σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assume:<br>    "∀(b::bexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧. bval b s<br>       ⟶ s ∈ ⟦tf_assume tf b σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assume_not:<br>    "∀(b::bexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧. ¬ bval b s<br>       ⟶ s ∈ ⟦tf_assume_not tf b σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_enter:<br>    "∀xs (es::aexp list) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧.<br>       bind_formals xs (map (λe. aval e s) es) (enter_state s)<br>         ∈ ⟦tf_enter tf xs es σ⟧"<br>... (2 more lines) | Sound transfer function: a domain_transfer tf that soundly over-approximates the concrete edge actions w.r.t. a sound_domain's concretization. Bundles the four per-action soundness obligations (assign / assume / assume-not / enter) as locale assumptions. Concrete domains discharge these once via `interpretation`. |
| type_synonym | `edge_tf_tree` | 853 | type_synonym ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree =<br>  "pp ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree" | An effectful_domain_transfer bundles per-action strategy tree producers. Each field takes the edge-action parameters plus the source program point u and returns a strategy tree that may QueryL/QueryG arbitrary unknowns, emit Side contributions to named globals, and ends with Answer carrying the local result. apply_etf dispatches on edge_action to the matching field. Unit-global effectful tree constructors live in TD_Side_CFG where restrict_local / restrict_global are defined. |
| type_synonym | `combine_tf_tree` | 862 | type_synonym ('g, 'd) combine_tf_tree =<br>  "pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree" | A combine (procedure return) tree producer takes the caller program point and the callee-exit program point and builds a tree that queries both their local unknowns (and any globals), emits Side contributions, and ends with the combined local result. Unlike an edge, it has two local inputs. |
| record | `effectful_domain_transfer` | 865 | record ('g, 'd) effectful_domain_transfer =<br>  etf_nop        :: "('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assign     :: "vname ⇒ aexp ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assume     :: "bexp  ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assume_not :: "bexp  ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_enter      :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_combine    :: "vname option ⇒ ('g, 'd) combine_tf_tree" |  |
| fun | `apply_etf` | 873 | fun apply_etf ::<br>  "('g, 'd) effectful_domain_transfer ⇒ edge_action ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "apply_etf etf EA_Nop           u = etf_nop etf u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Assign x a)  u = etf_assign etf x a u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Assume b)    u = etf_assume etf b u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_AssumeNot b) u = etf_assume_not etf b u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Ret e p) u =<br>     (case e of None ⇒ etf_nop etf u \| Some a ⇒ etf_assign etf ret_var a u)" |  |
| fun | `local_edge_action` | 884 | fun local_edge_action :: "edge_action ⇒ bool" where<br>  "local_edge_action EA_Nop = True"<br>\| "local_edge_action (EA_Assign x e) =<br>    ((~ is_global x) &amp; (~ aexp_mentions_global e))"<br>\| "local_edge_action (EA_Assume b) = (~ bexp_mentions_global b)"<br>\| "local_edge_action (EA_AssumeNot b) = (~ bexp_mentions_global b)"<br>\| "local_edge_action (EA_Ret e p) =<br>    (case e of None ⇒ True<br>     \| Some a ⇒ ((~ is_global ret_var) &amp; (~ aexp_mentions_global a)))" |  |
| primrec | `all_sides` | 927 | primrec all_sides ::<br>  "(pp, 'g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "all_sides (Answer d) σ = ⊥"<br>\| "all_sides (QueryL y f) σ = all_sides (f (σ (Inl y))) σ"<br>\| "all_sides (QueryG y f) σ = all_sides (f (σ (Inr y))) σ"<br>\| "all_sides (Side y d t) σ = d ⊔ all_sides t σ" | all_sides totals every Side contribution of a tree, regardless of the named global it targets. Unlike sides_of_rhs (which keeps a per-name map), this is a single finite join over the tree's Side nodes -- the data needed to reassemble the full post-state across arbitrarily many named globals without an infinite Sup. At 'g = unit every Side targets (), so all_sides coincides with sides_of_rhs _ (Inr ()) (all_sides_eq_sides_Inr_unit below). |
| definition | `etf_full` | 941 | definition etf_full ::<br>  "(pp, 'g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "etf_full t σ = traverse_rhs t σ ⊔ all_sides t σ" |  |
| definition | `glob_env` | 958 | definition glob_env ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "glob_env σ = abs_join_set (⊔) ⊥ ((λg. σ (Inr g)) ` UNIV)" | glob_env joins all named-global unknowns of sigma into a single abstract state: the full flow-insensitive global value seen at any point. With a finite global-name type the join is a finite fold (abs_join_set over the image of UNIV), so it is well defined in a non-complete bounded_semilattice_sup_bot. At 'g = unit it is the single pot sigma (Inr ()) (glob_env_unit), so it generalises the unit pipeline's read of the global unknown. |
| definition | `etf_collecting_full` | 1003 | definition etf_collecting_full ::<br>  "(pp, 'g::finite, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "etf_collecting_full t σ = etf_full t σ ⊔ glob_env σ" | Collecting soundness for local-only trees: `etf_full` carries locals; globals are restored by joining `glob_env`. |
| definition | `se_constraint_holds` | 1091 | definition se_constraint_holds ::<br>  "('x,'g,'d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x + 'g ⇒ 'd) ⇒ 'x ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "se_constraint_holds t σ u ≡<br>     traverse_rhs t σ ≤ σ (Inl u) ∧ sides_of_rhs t σ ≤ σ" | A valuation covers a strategy tree when it bounds both the tree's local answer and every side contribution produced during the same traversal. The local unknown bounds `traverse_rhs`; the complete valuation bounds `sides_of_rhs` pointwise. |
| definition | `inr_slot_locals_bot` | 1146 | definition inr_slot_locals_bot ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inr_slot_locals_bot σ =<br>     (∀g. ∀x. ¬ is_global x ⟶ σ (Inr g) x = bot)" |  |
| definition | `inl_slot_globals_bot` | 1160 | definition inl_slot_globals_bot ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inl_slot_globals_bot σ =<br>     (∀v. ∀x. is_global x ⟶ σ (Inl v) x = bot)" | Dual of `inr_slot_locals_bot`: every local program-point unknown carries bot in its global components. Holds for the effectful generator's solutions because each edge tree ends in a local-restricted Answer, so the local unknowns never accumulate globals. The keyed enter bound uses it to discard the caller's (bot) global part of a callee-entry frame. |
| definition | `inl_glob_le_glob_env` | 1175 | definition inl_glob_le_glob_env ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inl_glob_le_glob_env σ =<br>     (∀v. ∀x. is_global x ⟶ σ (Inl v) x ≤ glob_env σ x)" | The snapshot relaxation of `inl_slot_globals_bot`: a local unknown may carry globals, but each is bounded by the global environment. An analysis whose local Answers keep written globals @{emph `and`} publish the same values to the global slot satisfies it at any solution: every local-slot global sits below `glob_env`. This is exactly what the keyed enter bound needs: the caller's global part of a callee-entry frame is already covered by the globals. |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer` | 1185 | locale sound_effectful_transfer =<br>  fixes etf :: "('g::finite, 'a::sound_domain) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>  assumes etf_sound_nop:<br>    "∀u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧.<br>         s ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_nop etf u) σ⟧)"<br>  assumes etf_sound_assign:<br>    "∀x e u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧.<br>         s(x := aval e s) ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_assign etf x e u) σ⟧)"<br>  assumes etf_sound_assume:<br>    "∀(b::bexp) u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧. bval b s<br>       ⟶ s ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_assume etf b u) σ⟧)"<br>  assumes etf_sound_assume_not:<br>... (13 more lines) |  |
| definition | `local_formals` | 1230 | definition local_formals :: "vname list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "local_formals xs ↔ (∀x ∈ set xs. ¬ is_global x)" | Formal parameters name locals. `bind_formals_abs` writes each formal into the callee's fresh frame, so a formal naming a global would write a caller-local value into a global slot and break the frame bound. VIMP scoping supplies this; the CFG well-formedness discharges it at each enter edge. |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer_framed` | 1265 | locale sound_effectful_transfer_framed = sound_effectful_transfer +<br>  fixes fresh_frame :: "'a::sound_domain abs_state"<br>  assumes etf_enter_framed_le:<br>    "∀xs (es::aexp list) u σ.<br>       local_formals xs ⟶<br>       inr_slot_locals_bot σ ⟶ inl_slot_globals_bot σ ⟶<br>       etf_full (etf_enter etf xs es u) σ ≤ fresh_frame ⊔ glob_env σ" |  |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer_framed_le` | 1280 | locale sound_effectful_transfer_framed_le = sound_effectful_transfer +<br>  fixes fresh_frame :: "'a::sound_domain abs_state"<br>  assumes etf_enter_framed_glob_le:<br>    "∀xs (es::aexp list) u σ.<br>       local_formals xs ⟶<br>       inr_slot_locals_bot σ ⟶ inl_glob_le_glob_env σ ⟶<br>       etf_full (etf_enter etf xs es u) σ ≤ fresh_frame ⊔ glob_env σ" | The snapshot-compatible companion of `sound_effectful_transfer_framed`: the enter bound holds under the weaker premise `inl_glob_le_glob_env`, so an analysis whose local slots carry globals can still discharge it. Since `inl_slot_globals_bot` implies `inl_glob_le_glob_env`, this contract is stronger; `framed_le_imp_framed` recovers the publish contract. |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Ctx_Activation.thy

> The routed generator interprets solutions through `gamma_unit` with one shared global slot `Inr gk0`. This locale derives the edge and combine closure obligations from a `part_post_solution`, a sound D/G specification, the routing hooks, and a guarded solution reader. Concrete analyses supply the entry and callee-seed coverage obligations. The D/G layer transports abstract states at the caller, callee-result, and continuation slots. The trace semantics supplies the matched caller/callee relation, so this layer does not reconstruct activation pairing.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Framework.thy

> An analysis chooses a flow-sensitive answer domain `D` and a flow-insensitive side-effect domain `G`. The framework keeps them opaque and stores them in separate components of `dg_state`; it never copies a global component into a local answer. `dg_edge_tree` and `dg_combine_tree` only project and repack those components, so their construction is independent of the concrete domains.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `unit_step` | 17 | definition unit_step ::<br>  "('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state × 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "unit_step f d g = (let res = f (d ⊔ g) in (restrict_global res, restrict_local res))" |  |
| datatype | `dg_state` | 36 | datatype ('l, 'g) dg_state = DG (locals: 'l) (globs: 'g)<br><br>instantiation dg_state :: (ord, ord) ord<br>begin | The solver's single value type must order local and global halves componentwise. Raw pairs cannot: `CFG_Def` imports `HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder`, so `'l × 'g` already carries the lexicographic order everywhere in this repository, and the componentwise `HOL-Library.Product_Order` instances clash with it. `dg_state` is the componentwise-ordered copy of `('l, 'g) split_state`. |
| definition | `less_eq_dg_state` | 41 | definition less_eq_dg_state :: "('a, 'b) dg_state ⇒ ('a, 'b) dg_state ⇒ bool" where<br>  "less_eq_dg_state d1 d2 = (locals d1 ≤ locals d2 ∧ globs d1 ≤ globs d2)" |  |

QueryG () (λg.<br>       Side () (DG bot (fst (step (locals d) (globs g))))<br>         (Answer (DG (snd (step (locals d) (globs g)))  bot))))" | Two independent opaque domains. An analysis provides `step : D ⇒ G ⇒ G × D` (transfer plus publication); the framework reads the local unknown's `D` and the global slot's `G`, and transports the step's `Side : G` and `Answer : D`. Slot packing (`DG _ bot` / `DG bot _`) is the encoding of the two-typed unknown space into the solver's single value type; the framework never looks inside `D` or `G`. |
| definition | `dg_combine_tree` | 208 | definition dg_combine_tree ::<br>  "(vname option ⇒ 'dl::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dg × 'dl)<br>   ⇒ vname option ⇒ pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, unit, ('dl, 'dg) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "dg_combine_tree comb dst cc ex =<br>     QueryL cc (λdc. QueryL ex (λde. QueryG () (λg.<br>       Side () (DG bot (fst (comb dst (locals dc) (locals de) (globs g))))<br>         (Answer (DG (snd (comb dst (locals dc) (locals de) (globs g))) bot)))))" | Procedure-return combine: two `D` inputs (caller, callee exit), one `G`. |
| record | `dg_spec` | 242 | record ('dl, 'dg) dg_spec =<br>  dgs_nop        :: "'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assign     :: "vname ⇒ aexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assume     :: "bexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assume_not :: "bexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_enter      :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_combine_env    :: "'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_combine_assign :: "vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl ⇒ 'dg × 'dl" | Procedure-return combine is split the same way Goblint's `Spec` splits it: an environment merge (`dgs_combine_env`, caller-local + callee-exit-local + global, no destination) followed by a return-value assign (`dgs_combine_assign`, reads the callee-exit's return slot, writes the destination, and packages the two-typed `('dg, 'dl)` answer). `'dl`/`'dg` stay fully opaque -- the framework does not assume either field can share a generic assign the way the flat layer's `abs_state`-typed `combine_assign_abs` can, so both halves are analysis-supplied. |
| definition | `dgs_combine` | 255 | definition dgs_combine ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>where<br>  "dgs_combine S dst dc de g = dgs_combine_assign S dst de g (dgs_combine_env S dc de g)" | The composed combine, in the pre-split curried shape every existing caller already uses fully applied (`dgs_combine S dst dc de g`). Kept as a plain definition, not a record field, so the split above is the single source of truth and this cannot drift out of sync with it. |
| fun | `dg_spec_step` | 260 | fun dg_spec_step ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg, 'z) dg_spec_scheme ⇒ edge_action ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>where<br>  "dg_spec_step S EA_Nop           = dgs_nop S"<br>\| "dg_spec_step S (EA_Assign x e)  = dgs_assign S x e"<br>\| "dg_spec_step S (EA_Assume b)    = dgs_assume S b"<br>\| "dg_spec_step S (EA_AssumeNot b) = dgs_assume_not S b"<br>\| "dg_spec_step S (EA_Ret e p) =<br>     (case e of None ⇒ dgs_nop S \| Some a ⇒ dgs_assign S ret_var a)" |  |

| definition | `dg_spec_combine_tree` | 276 | definition dg_spec_combine_tree ::<br>  "('dl::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'dg::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) dg_spec<br>   ⇒ vname option ⇒ pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, unit, ('dl, 'dg) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "dg_spec_combine_tree S dst cc ex = dg_combine_tree (dgs_combine S) dst cc ex" |  |

The unit assign reconstitutes the full state from that packaging, writes the callee-exit's return slot, and re-splits -- matching `combine_collect_abs` exactly once composed. |
| definition | `unit_combine_step_assign` | 302 | definition unit_combine_step_assign ::<br>  "vname option ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state × 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state × 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "unit_combine_step_assign dst de g merged =<br>     (let res = combine_assign_abs dst ((de ⊔ g) ret_var) (fst merged ⊔ snd merged)<br>      in (restrict_global res, restrict_local res))" |  |

| definition | `side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg` | 393 | definition side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg ::<br>  "(cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × edge_action) list)<br>   ⇒ ('c ⇒ 'k)<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ ((pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ call_action ⇒ pp ⇒ pp<br>        ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) strategy_tree)<br>   ⇒ ((pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ pp<br>        ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) strategy_tree list)<br>   ⇒ cfg<br>   ⇒ ('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'h::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) dg_spec<br>   ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'h<br>   ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg pred_sel gkey route cmb extra g S bot0 s0d s0g =<br>     (λ(v, c).<br>... (8 more lines) | The one context-generic generator. Enter handling is routed by three hooks so that a monovariant and a context-sensitive analysis are both instances: • `pred_sel` selects the intra predecessors folded as Answers into a node. The monovariant instance uses `intra_predecessor_list` over `intra`; a callee entry over `calls` merges into the single callee context, while the context-sensitive instance instead publishes routed callee seeds. • `cmb` is the procedure-return combine tree (already fully abstract: the context-sensitive instance reads the callee exit under the routed context). • `extra c v` supplies additional per-node trees folded after the intra and combine trees. Their Answers add to the node's local accumulator and their `Side` effects are collected --- this is where a frame-entry seed *read* (a `QueryG` of the incoming seed slot) and the caller-side call-entry *publication* (a routed `Side` to the callee seed slot) live. The monovariant instance supplies `λ_ _. []`. |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Soundness.thy

> Collecting soundness stated directly over an analysis's two domains `D` and `G`. The analysis supplies `gammaDG`, the joint meaning of a per-point Answer and the shared Side fact. Independent analyses use the intersection `gamma_dg d g = [[d]] Int [[g]]`; the homogeneous unit analysis uses `gamma_unit d g = [[d Sup g]]` because its transfer merges the slots. The locale `sound_dg_spec` assumes only monotonicity of `gammaDG` and semantic soundness of the analysis's edge and combine operations. It derives the structural post-fixpoint from the heterogeneous generator, then applies the collecting-semantics post-fixpoint theorem directly. No homogeneous equation system or representation transport appears in this proof.

| locale | `sound_dg_spec` | 135 | locale sound_dg_spec =<br>  fixes S :: "('D::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot,<br>                'G::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) dg_spec"<br>    and gammaDG :: "'D ⇒ 'G ⇒ store set"<br>  assumes gammaDG_mono:<br>      "⟦d ≤ d'; g ≤ g'⟧ ⟹<br>        gammaDG d g ⊆ gammaDG d' g'"<br>    and step_sound:<br>      "edge_collect a (gammaDG d g) ⊆<br>        (case dg_spec_step S a d g of<br>           (g', d') ⇒ gammaDG d' g')"<br>    and combine_sound:<br>      "⟦s ∈ gammaDG dc g; t ∈ gammaDG de g⟧ ⟹<br>        combine_collect dst s t ∈<br>          (case dgs_combine S dst dc de g of<br>... ())<br>         (map_ltree (λw. (w, ctx))<br>           (dg_spec_combine_tree S dst cc ex)))" |  |

| definition | `dg_postfix` | 291 | definition dg_postfix ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ 'D ⇒ 'G<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit + unit ⇒<br>        ('D, 'G) dg_state)<br>   ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "dg_postfix g s0d s0g sigma ↔<br>     s0d ≤ dg_D sigma (cfg_entry g) ∧<br>     s0g ≤ dg_G sigma ∧<br>     (∀u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟶<br>        snd (dg_spec_step S a (dg_D sigma u) (dg_G sigma))<br>          ≤ dg_D sigma v) ∧<br>     (∀u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟶<br>        fst (dg_spec_step S a (dg_D sigma u) (dg_G sigma))<br>          ≤ dg_G sigma) ∧<br>... (12 more lines) | `dg_postfix` is the DG-specific instance of a semantic post-fixpoint: `sigma` dominates the seed on both projections, and every specification transfer --- `dg_spec_step` on an intra edge, `dgs_enter` on a call entry, `dgs_combine` on a call return --- is bounded on both projections by `sigma` at its target. The eight conjuncts are the two seed bounds plus a D/G pair for each of the three transfer kinds; each has its own named projection lemma below (`dg_postfix_entryD`, `dg_postfix_edgeD`, ...) so callers never navigate the conjunction positionally. |
| definition | `indep_dg_spec` | 784 | definition indep_dg_spec ::<br>  "'d::sound_domain domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'g::sound_domain domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ ('d abs_state, 'g abs_state) dg_spec"<br>where<br>  "indep_dg_spec tfD tfG = ⦇<br>    dgs_nop        = (λd g. (apply_tf tfG EA_Nop g, apply_tf tfD EA_Nop d)),<br>    dgs_assign     = (λx e d g. (apply_tf tfG (EA_Assign x e) g,<br>                                 apply_tf tfD (EA_Assign x e) d)),<br>    dgs_assume     = (λb d g. (apply_tf tfG (EA_Assume b) g,<br>                               apply_tf tfD (EA_Assume b) d)),<br>    dgs_assume_not = (λb d g. (apply_tf tfG (EA_AssumeNot b) g,<br>                               apply_tf tfD (EA_AssumeNot b) d)),<br>    dgs_enter      = (λxs es d g. (tf_enter tfG xs es g,<br>                                   tf_enter tfD xs es d)),<br>... (5 more lines) | The record an independent-transfer analysis denotes: every edge action advances each slot by its own transfer, the combine is structural in each slot. Any two sound transfers yield a `sound_dg_spec` instance; at `tfD = tfG` this is the homogeneous single-domain analysis as the diagonal. |
| definition | `gamma_unit` | 889 | definition gamma_unit ::<br>  "'a::sound_domain abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ store set"<br>where<br>  "gamma_unit d g = ⟦d ⊔ g⟧" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Transfer_Combinators.thy

> `dgs_enter` and `dgs_combine` already return the `'dg × 'dl` pair the framework's split demands: the global side effect and the local answer. Equations that call them read as `fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` / `snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` at every call site, which names the projection instead of the role. These abbreviations name the role; like the generic `Strategy_Tree_Combinators`, each is a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` still exposes exactly `fst`/`snd` applied to `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| abbreviation | `enter_global` | 18 | abbreviation enter_global ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg"<br>where<br>  "enter_global S fs as d g ≡ fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)" | `dgs_enter` and `dgs_combine` already return the `'dg × 'dl` pair the framework's split demands: the global side effect and the local answer. Equations that call them read as `fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` / `snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` at every call site, which names the projection instead of the role. These abbreviations name the role; like the generic `Strategy_Tree_Combinators`, each is a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` still exposes exactly `fst`/`snd` applied to `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine`. |
| abbreviation | `enter_local` | 23 | abbreviation enter_local ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dl"<br>where<br>  "enter_local S fs as d g ≡ snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `combine_global` | 28 | abbreviation combine_global ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg"<br>where<br>  "combine_global S dst dc de g ≡ fst (dgs_combine S dst dc de g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `combine_local` | 33 | abbreviation combine_local ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dl"<br>where<br>  "combine_local S dst dc de g ≡ snd (dgs_combine S dst dc de g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_global` | 66 | abbreviation publish_global where<br>  "publish_global key x ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) (answer (DG bot bot))" | Every value a DG equation reads or publishes is a `('d, 'd) dg_state` pair (`DG`), with the unused half fixed to `bot` by convention: a global-key publication -- the one shared `gk0` slot, or a routed seed slot, both living in the same global-key space `'g` -- carries its payload in the `globs` half; a local-key answer carries its payload in the `locals` half (`routed_extra`'s frame-entry read and `routed_cmb`'s final answer, both in `Routed_Context`, return the analysis's own local contribution this way). `publish_global`, `publish_seed`, and `answer_local` apply that convention so an equation never writes `DG`, `fst`, or `snd` directly -- each is still a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` exposes exactly the `DG` term it names. `publish_global` and `publish_seed` are `depend_on` under the same `DG` convention, named for which global-key role the publication plays; a routed context-sensitive analysis (`Routed_Context`) uses one of each per call. Both are value-producing (no trailing continuation): as a `do`-block statement (no `←`), the published side effect is what matters and the answer -- `DG bot bot`, the same neutral value every `_cont` caller already supplied by hand -- is discarded by the bind. The `_cont` forms keep the explicit continuation for direct, non-`do` use. No explicit type signature is given -- as with `enter_global`/`combine_global`, an annotated signature forcing both `DG` halves to one shared type variable over-constrains unification wherever the caller and callee sides are inferred independently. |
| abbreviation | `publish_seed` | 69 | abbreviation publish_seed where<br>  "publish_seed key x ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) (answer (DG bot bot))" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_global_cont` | 72 | abbreviation publish_global_cont where<br>  "publish_global_cont key x cont ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) cont" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_seed_cont` | 75 | abbreviation publish_seed_cont where<br>  "publish_seed_cont key x cont ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) cont" |  |
| abbreviation | `answer_local` | 78 | abbreviation answer_local where<br>  "answer_local x ≡ answer (DG x bot)" |  |
| abbreviation | `with_call` | 89 | abbreviation with_call ::<br>  "call_action ⇒ (vname option ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'a) ⇒ 'a"<br>where<br>  "with_call ca f ≡ case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ f dst fs as"<br><br>end | `call_action` (`CFG_Def.thy`) has one constructor, so matching it is a total destructure, not a partial case split. `with_call` names that destructure once per call site instead of repeating \<open>case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ ...\<close> at every `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine` call the site makes. |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/DG/Routed_Context.thy

> `dg_ctx_activation` already discharges EDGE (`dg_ctx_act_edge`) generically off the post-solution, independent of `route`/`cmb`/`extra`: intra edges never route. Its COMB analogue (`dg_ctx_act_comb_covered`) is generic in the same sense but still takes the tree's contribution as an assumption (`bound`) rather than deriving it, because `cmb` is an unconstrained parameter: a context-sensitive analysis whose entry-seed publication and return combine are hand-written has to rederive that same routing argument itself. This theory fixes `cmb` and `extra` to one canonical shape, parametric only in a routing function `route` and a seed-key injection `seed_key`, and discharges CALL and COMB as theorems of that shape: a k-call-string or a partial-tabulation context becomes an interpretation of this locale, not a second proof development.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `routed_cmb` | 31 | definition routed_cmb ::<br>  "('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'd) dg_spec ⇒ 'k<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ 'c ⇒ call_action ⇒ pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'd) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "routed_cmb S gk0 route ctx ca cc ex =<br>     with_call ca (λdst _ _. do {<br>       caller_state ← read_local (cc, ctx);<br>       let ctx' = route cc ctx (locals caller_state) ca;<br>       callee_state ← read_local (ex, ctx');<br>       globals_state ← read_global gk0;<br>       let caller = locals caller_state;<br>       let callee = locals callee_state;<br>       let globals = globs globals_state;<br>       publish_global gk0 (combine_global S dst caller callee globals);<br>... (2 more lines) | Parameter order matches `dg_ctx_activation`'s `cmb` calling convention: the generator supplies `route` as `cmb`'s own first argument (`cmb route c ca cc ex` in `side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg`), so `routed_cmb S gk0`, closing over only the spec and the shared slot, is the value that instantiates `cmb`. |
| definition | `routed_extra` | 54 | definition routed_extra ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ ('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'd) dg_spec<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'k) ⇒ 'k<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ 'c ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'd) dg_state) strategy_tree list"<br>where<br>  "routed_extra g S seed_key gk0 route ctx v =<br>     (case v of FunctionEntry _ ⇒<br>        [do { seed_state ← read_global (seed_key v ctx); answer_local (globs seed_state) }]<br>       \| _ ⇒ [])<br>     @ map (λ(w, ca, k).<br>             with_call ca (λdst fs as. do {<br>               entry_state ← read_local (v, ctx);<br>               globals_state ← read_global gk0;<br>               let entry = locals entry_state;<br>... (7 more lines) | Per node `v`: if `v` is a callee entry, read back its own routed seed; for every outgoing call from `v`, publish the entered store into the callee's routed seed slot, reading the shared global `gk0` the same way `routed_cmb` does. The seed key is injected through an arbitrary `seed_key` function rather than fixed to any particular global-key type, so the same shape serves any routing policy. |
| locale | `routed_context` | 91 | locale routed_context =<br>  dg_ctx_activation S g gk0 route "routed_cmb S gk0" "routed_extra g S seed_key gk0"<br>    bot0 s0d s0g sigma vars x0 sg<br>  for S :: "('a::sound_domain abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_spec"<br>    and g gk0 route bot0 s0d s0g sigma vars x0 sg<br>    and seed_key :: "pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'k" +<br>  fixes enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c"<br>  assumes finC: "finite (calls g)"<br>    and seed_key_ne_gk0: "⋀p ctx. seed_key p ctx ≠ gk0"<br>    and route_enterc_agree:<br>    "⋀u ctx dst pars args p cont s.<br>       (u, ctx) ∈ vars<br>       ⟹ (u, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>       ⟹ s ∈ gamma_unit (locals (sigma (Inl (u, ctx)))) (globs (sigma (Inr gk0)))<br>       ⟹ route u ctx (locals (sigma (Inl (u, ctx)))) (CallEdge dst pars args)<br>... (16 more lines) | Beyond `dg_ctx_activation`'s parameters: `seed_key` injects a routed `(pp, 'c)` pair into the global-key space; `enterc` is the trace-semantic context function keying the activation-local collecting semantics; and `route_enterc_agree` is the one agreement that cannot be discharged generically: at a real call edge, the equation-level `route`, evaluated on an abstract caller-local value, must agree with the semantic `enterc` on every concrete entered store the value concretizes to. Restricting the call action to an edge of `g` (rather than quantifying over every value of the `call_action` type) matches how CALL and COMB below only ever invoke this fact at a matched edge, and keeps the obligation provable for an abstract domain that is exact on edges actually present in the program without being exact everywhere. This is a per-instance proof obligation, not a locale theorem. |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Context/Read/TD_Side_Eff_Keyed_Gen.thy

> Global-key relabelling for strategy trees, the `QueryG` / `Side` analogue of `map_ltree`. The heterogeneous D/G generator relabels the local unknowns of a per-edge tree by context (`map_ltree`) and routes its global writes to the context's keyed slot with `map_gtree`; `traverse_rhs_map_gtree` shows the latter commutes with the denotation under the matching `map_sum`-pullback of the global slots, exactly as `traverse_rhs_map_ltree` does on the local side.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| primrec | `map_gtree` | 18 | primrec map_gtree ::<br>  "('g ⇒ 'h) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x, 'h, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "map_gtree r (Answer d) = Answer d"<br>\| "map_gtree r (QueryL y f) = QueryL y (λd. map_gtree r (f d))"<br>\| "map_gtree r (QueryG y f) = QueryG (r y) (λd. map_gtree r (f d))"<br>\| "map_gtree r (Side y d t) = Side (r y) d (map_gtree r t)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Core/Strategy_Tree_Combinators.thy

> The verified TD solver's instruction set is four constructors on `('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree`: `QueryL` reads a local unknown, `QueryG` reads a global unknown, `Side` publishes a value under a global key and continues, `Answer` yields the local result. Equation authors construct trees directly with these constructors, so an equation such as ``combine the caller state, the routed callee state, and the globals'' reads as nested nameless lambdas over `QueryL`/`QueryG` rather than as the combine it computes. The abbreviations below rename the four constructors to their equation-authoring role. Each is a plain syntax translation, not a new constant: a term written with the renamed form parses to and unfolds as exactly the constructor term it abbreviates, so every existing lemma about `QueryL`/`QueryG`/`Side`/`Answer` -- and every proof that unfolds an equation's `_def` and pattern-matches on those constructors -- applies unchanged.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| abbreviation | `answer` | 26 | abbreviation answer :: "'d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "answer d ≡ Answer d" | The verified TD solver's instruction set is four constructors on `('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree`: `QueryL` reads a local unknown, `QueryG` reads a global unknown, `Side` publishes a value under a global key and continues, `Answer` yields the local result. Equation authors construct trees directly with these constructors, so an equation such as ``combine the caller state, the routed callee state, and the globals'' reads as nested nameless lambdas over `QueryL`/`QueryG` rather than as the combine it computes. The abbreviations below rename the four constructors to their equation-authoring role. Each is a plain syntax translation, not a new constant: a term written with the renamed form parses to and unfolds as exactly the constructor term it abbreviates, so every existing lemma about `QueryL`/`QueryG`/`Side`/`Answer` -- and every proof that unfolds an equation's `_def` and pattern-matches on those constructors -- applies unchanged. |
| abbreviation | `read_local` | 40 | abbreviation read_local :: "'x ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "read_local key ≡ QueryL key answer" | `read_local`/`read_global` are value-producing: the queried value itself is the tree's answer, so `seqcomp_tree` (bind for this monad, `Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy`, given `do`-notation via `Strategy_Tree_Do.thy`'s `adhoc_overloading`) sequences a read directly as `do { d ← read_local key; ... }`. Unfolding `seqcomp_tree`'s two defining equations on a bound read reduces it to exactly the `_cont` form below, so proofs that need the flat constructor shape add `seqcomp_tree.simps` to their simp set once, then proceed as before. |
| abbreviation | `read_global` | 43 | abbreviation read_global :: "'g ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "read_global key ≡ QueryG key answer" |  |
| abbreviation | `read_local_cont` | 52 | abbreviation read_local_cont ::<br>  "'x ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "read_local_cont key k ≡ QueryL key k" | `_cont` suffix: the original continuation-passing shape, kept for direct, single-step low-level use (no bind/do-notation needed) and as the primitive `read_local`/`read_global` themselves compile down to. |
| abbreviation | `read_global_cont` | 57 | abbreviation read_global_cont ::<br>  "'g ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "read_global_cont key k ≡ QueryG key k" |  |
| abbreviation | `depend_on` | 74 | abbreviation depend_on ::<br>  "'g ⇒ 'd ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "depend_on key val cont ≡ Side key val cont"<br><br>end | `depend_on key val cont` publishes `val` as a side effect on the global unknown `key` and continues as `cont`. The name reflects the direction an equation author reasons in: ``this equation also depends on -- contributes to -- `key`'', not the solver-internal notion of a queued side write. Its trailing continuation is what a do-block statement (no `←`) needs, so `depend_on` itself keeps this shape rather than gaining a value-producing twin: statement-form combinators built on it (`publish_global`, `publish_seed`, `DG_Transfer_Combinators.thy`) supply `answer bot` as that continuation. |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Core/Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy

> seqcomp_tree sequences two strategy trees: run t, pass its Answer value to continuation k. Monadic bind for the strategy tree monad; the key combinator for building effectful IP folds from per-edge trees.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| primrec | `seqcomp_tree` | 13 | primrec seqcomp_tree ::<br>  "('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree)<br>   ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "seqcomp_tree (Answer v)   k = k v"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (QueryL u f) k = QueryL u  (λd. seqcomp_tree (f d) k)"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (QueryG g f) k = QueryG g  (λd. seqcomp_tree (f d) k)"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (Side g v t) k = Side g v  (seqcomp_tree t k)" | seqcomp_tree sequences two strategy trees: run t, pass its Answer value to continuation k. Monadic bind for the strategy tree monad; the key combinator for building effectful IP folds from per-edge trees. |
| definition | `static_deps` | 92 | definition static_deps :: "('x, 'g, 'd::order_bot) strategy_tree ⇒ bool" where<br>  "static_deps t ↔ (∀σ1 σ2. dep_aux σ1 t = dep_aux σ2 t)" | static_deps t: the set of unknowns queried in t does not depend on the environment. The TD solver's mono_deps precondition needs this. It holds for trees whose QueryL/QueryG skeleton is fixed and only the Side/Answer values vary with the environment (the safe invariant for effectful TF trees). |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Core/TD_Side_CFG.thy

> Generic base for the side-effecting interprocedural solver. A locals/globals split on abstract states: restrict_local / restrict_global keep one component (the other set to bot), so their join recovers the original state. side_env combines the local unknown at a program point with the single global unknown. The interprocedural strategy tree and transfer functions live in TD_Side_Tree; monotonicity and solver preconditions live in TD_Side_Eff_Bounds and TD_Side_Eff_Cone_Lemmas.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `restrict_local` | 25 | definition restrict_local ::<br>  "'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "restrict_local σ = (λx. if is_global x then bot else σ x)" |  |
| definition | `restrict_global` | 29 | definition restrict_global ::<br>  "'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "restrict_global σ = (λx. if is_global x then σ x else bot)" |  |
| definition | `side_env` | 143 | definition side_env ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) =&gt; pp =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "side_env σ v = σ (Inl v) ⊔ glob_env σ" |  |
| definition | `side_env_g` | 157 | definition side_env_g ::<br>  "(pp + 'g =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) =&gt; 'g =&gt; pp =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "side_env_g σ g v = σ (Inl v) ⊔ σ (Inr g)" |  |
| definition | `unit_edge_tree` | 183 | definition unit_edge_tree ::<br>  "('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state)<br>   =&gt; (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "unit_edge_tree f u =<br>     QueryL u (λsu. QueryG () (λg.<br>       let res = f (su ⊔ g) in<br>       Side () (restrict_global res)<br>         (Answer (restrict_local res))))" | Unit-global effectful trees query the local program point and the unit global slot, compute one abstract post-state, then split that result between the local Answer and the global Side contribution consumed by TD_side. |
| definition | `unit_combine_tree` | 196 | definition unit_combine_tree ::<br>  "vname option =&gt; pp =&gt; pp<br>   =&gt; (pp, unit, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "unit_combine_tree dst cc ex =<br>     QueryL cc (λsc. QueryL ex (λse. QueryG () (λg.<br>       let res = combine_collect_abs dst (sc ⊔ g) (se ⊔ g) in<br>       Side () (restrict_global res)<br>         (Answer (restrict_local res)))))" |  |
| definition | `local_bot_on_locals` | 267 | definition local_bot_on_locals ::<br>  "'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "local_bot_on_locals g ↔ (∀x. ¬ is_global x ⟶ g x = bot)" | When an edge transfer preserves globals and only reads locals, the tree can query the source local unknown without `QueryG`. Soundness and post-fixpoint bounds use `etf_full` joined with `glob_env`. |
| definition | `local_edge_invariant` | 330 | definition local_edge_invariant ::<br>  "('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "local_edge_invariant f ↔<br>     (∀su g. local_bot_on_locals g ⟶<br>        f (restrict_local su ⊔ g) =<br>        restrict_local (f (restrict_local su)) ⊔ g)" |  |
| definition | `local_edge_tree` | 392 | definition local_edge_tree ::<br>  "('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>   ⇒ (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "local_edge_tree f u =<br>     QueryL u (λsu. Answer<br>       (restrict_local (f (restrict_local su)) ⊔ restrict_global su))" |  |
| definition | `unit_etf_of_transfer` | 554 | definition unit_etf_of_transfer ::<br>  "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ (unit, 'a) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>where<br>  "unit_etf_of_transfer tf = ⦇<br>    etf_nop        = (λu. unit_edge_tree (apply_tf tf EA_Nop) u),<br>    etf_assign     = (λx e u. unit_edge_tree (apply_tf tf (EA_Assign x e)) u),<br>    etf_assume     = (λb u. unit_edge_tree (apply_tf tf (EA_Assume b)) u),<br>    etf_assume_not = (λb u. unit_edge_tree (apply_tf tf (EA_AssumeNot b)) u),<br>    etf_enter      = (λxs es u. unit_edge_tree (tf_enter tf xs es) u),<br>    etf_combine    = unit_combine_tree<br>  ⦈" |  |
| definition | `mixed_etf_edge_tree` | 566 | definition mixed_etf_edge_tree ::<br>  "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ edge_action ⇒ (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "mixed_etf_edge_tree tf a u =<br>    (if local_edge_action a then local_edge_tree (apply_tf tf a) u<br>     else unit_edge_tree (apply_tf tf a) u)" |  |
| definition | `mixed_etf_of_transfer` | 573 | definition mixed_etf_of_transfer ::<br>  "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ (unit, 'a) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>where<br>  "mixed_etf_of_transfer tf = ⦇<br>    etf_nop        = mixed_etf_edge_tree tf EA_Nop,<br>    etf_assign     = (λx e. mixed_etf_edge_tree tf (EA_Assign x e)),<br>    etf_assume     = (λb. mixed_etf_edge_tree tf (EA_Assume b)),<br>    etf_assume_not = (λb. mixed_etf_edge_tree tf (EA_AssumeNot b)),<br>    etf_enter      = (λxs es u. unit_edge_tree (tf_enter tf xs es) u),<br>    etf_combine    = unit_combine_tree<br>  ⦈" |  |

### src/Analysis/Generic/Solver/Core/TD_Side_Eff_Bounds.thy

> Monotonicity of the effectful equation system for an arbitrary etf follows from a per-tree contract: each edge, enter and combine tree is monotone in the environment. A genuinely effectful etf supplies this contract directly, e.g. via seqcomp_mono on its QueryL/QueryG/Side construction. The per-tree contract is stated on traverse_rhs of the trees the fold composes (apply_etf etf a u for ordinary edges, etf_enter etf fs as cl for call entries, etf_combine etf dst cc ex for caller continuations). Everything here is generic in the named-global type 'g; the global-side closure bounds (etf_combined_le_eff / etf_enter_combined_le_eff / etf_combine_combined_le_eff) route the per-name side bound through glob_env, so they require 'g::finite.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `strip_inr_globals` | 582 | definition strip_inr_globals ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state)<br>   ⇒ pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "strip_inr_globals σ k = (case k of Inl p ⇒ σ (Inl p) \| Inr g ⇒ restrict_global (σ (Inr g)))" |  |
