## CFG

### src/CFG/CFG_Def.thy

> A `cfg` is an interprocedural control-flow graph over two disjoint transition relations, one per phenomenon: • `intra` --- ordinary context-preserving flow, labelled by an `edge_action` that is a total within-context store transformer. Executed by `edge_step`. • `calls` --- context-crossing calls, labelled by a `call_action` and carrying the call-site, the callee entry node, and the continuation node. Nodes are procedure-aware: `FunctionEntry p` and `FunctionResult p` are first-class nodes, not keyed side slots. There is no global exit node and no call/return matching side relation: a call edge already names its callee entry and its continuation, and a return is an ordinary intra edge into `FunctionResult p`. Calls carry incompatible typing from intra flow, so `edge_step` has no call case and `intra` can never carry a call --- by typing, not by a side condition. Translation from VIMP to CFG and the collecting/equation layers ride on this structure.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `cfg_node` | 27 | datatype cfg_node =<br>    Statement nat<br>  \| FunctionEntry pname<br>  \| FunctionResult pname<br><br>instance cfg_node :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype |  |
| datatype | `edge_action` | 52 | datatype edge_action =<br>    EA_Nop<br>  \| EA_Assign   vname aexp<br>  \| EA_Assume   bexp<br>  \| EA_AssumeNot bexp<br>  \| EA_Ret      "aexp option" pname | `edge_action` labels intra flow. It has no call constructor: every action is a total store transformer within one activation, so an intra edge can never denote a call. A `EA_Ret e p` writes the return value into `ret_var` in the callee's own context; its graph target is `FunctionResult p` (enforced by `wf_cfg`), which is why return summarisation is ordinary predecessor folding over `FunctionResult p`. `call_action` labels call edges. `CallEdge dst formals args` records the caller destination variable, the callee's formal parameter names, and the actual arguments; the callee identity and the continuation are the two target nodes of the `calls` tuple, not payload of the action. Carrying `formals` on the edge lets the caller-side entry transfer `call_enter` bind actuals to formals without consulting a procedure table, keeping the trace kernel independent of the source declaration environment. |
| datatype | `call_action` | 59 | datatype call_action =<br>    CallEdge "vname option" "vname list" "aexp list"<br><br>instance edge_action :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype<br><br>instance call_action :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype |  |
| record | `cfg` | 77 | record cfg =<br>  intra     :: "(cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set"<br>  calls     :: "(cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>  cfg_entry :: cfg_node | `calls` is a four-place relation `(call_site, act, callee_entry, continuation)`: the callee entry and the continuation are ordinary nodes, kept explicit so no matching side relation is needed. The flat (call-free) CFG is exactly the `calls = {}` fragment, so every fact quantified over `intra` is verbatim a flat-CFG fact. |
| fun | `edge_step` | 87 | fun edge_step :: "edge_action ⇒ store ⇒ store option" where<br>  "edge_step EA_Nop s = Some s"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Assign x a) s = Some (s(x := aval a s))"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Assume b) s = (if bval b s then Some s else None)"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_AssumeNot b) s = (if bval b s then None else Some s)"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Ret e p) s =<br>     Some (s(ret_var := (case e of None ⇒ s ret_var \| Some a ⇒ aval a s)))" | `edge_step` is the single primitive semantics of an intra action. It is defined for every constructor and has no call case; guards are the only source of `None`. |
| definition | `cfg_intra_step` | 109 | definition cfg_intra_step :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_intra_step g p q ↔<br>     (∃a. (fst p, a, fst q) ∈ intra g ∧ edge_step a (snd p) = Some (snd q))" | One intra transition on a `(node, store)` pair. `intra_path` is its `star`, so reflexivity, transitivity and the induction rule come from `HOL-IMP.Star` rather than from a bespoke closure. The name avoids `intra_step`, which is the *source*-level `pstep` fragment. |
| abbreviation | `intra_path` | 113 | abbreviation intra_path :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "intra_path g ≡ star (cfg_intra_step g)" |  |
| definition | `combine_collect` | 148 | definition combine_collect :: "vname option ⇒ store ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "combine_collect dst s t = combine_assign dst (t ret_var) (combine_states s t)" | Return-value rehydration at the caller: write the callee's `ret_var` into the destination over the combined store (callee globals, caller locals). It is fixed by the call's destination `dst`, which the `CallEdge` already records --- no side lookup. |
| definition | `call_enter` | 162 | definition call_enter :: "call_action ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "call_enter ca s =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst pars actuals ⇒<br>        bind_formals pars (map (λe. aval e s) actuals) (enter_state s))" | Caller-side entry transfer at a call. The actuals are evaluated in the caller store, the callee locals are reset (`enter_state`, globals preserved), and the resulting values are bound to the callee formals. All payload comes from the `CallEdge`, so the transfer needs no procedure table. This is exactly the callee-entry store produced by the source `pstep` `Call` rule (see `call_enter_eq_source_call_store`). |
| definition | `intra_successors` | 180 | definition intra_successors :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "intra_successors g u = {v. ∃a. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_nodes` | 190 | definition cfg_nodes :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "cfg_nodes g =<br>     {u. ∃a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>     ∪ {v. ∃u a. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>     ∪ {u. ∃act ce after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {ce. ∃u act after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {after. ∃u act ce. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {cfg_entry g}" | `cfg_nodes` is reconstructed from the endpoints of `intra`, every node occurring in `calls`, and `cfg_entry`. It is not a record field. |
| definition | `flat_cfg` | 201 | definition flat_cfg :: "cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "flat_cfg g ↔ calls g = {}" |  |
| definition | `wf_cfg` | 209 | definition wf_cfg :: "cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "wf_cfg g ↔<br>     (∀u act ce after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g ⟶ (∃p. ce = FunctionEntry p))<br>   ∧ (∀u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟶ (∀p. v ≠ FunctionEntry p))<br>   ∧ (∀u e p v. (u, EA_Ret e p, v) ∈ intra g ⟶ v = FunctionResult p)" | `wf_cfg` is structural only: call edges enter procedure-entry nodes; no intra edge enters a procedure-entry node (so a callee is reached only across a call); and a return action lands on the matching procedure result. No compiler-correctness or source-semantic property is included. |

### src/CFG/CFG_Prune.thy

> `cfg_succ_rel` is the derived structural dependency graph the analysis pruning and cone proofs run on --- not the concrete execution relation. It has four sources, induced by `intra g` and `calls g`: • INTRA: an ordinary edge `(u, a, v) ∈ intra g` gives `u → v` (ordinary flow). • ENTRY: a call `(c, ca, FunctionEntry p, k) ∈ calls g` gives `c → FunctionEntry p` --- the callee entry's abstract state depends on the caller state routed through `etf_enter`. • COMB_CALLER: the same call gives `c → k` --- the continuation depends on the saved caller state via `etf_combine`. This is not a concrete execution edge; execution does not skip the callee. • COMB_RESULT: the same call gives `FunctionResult p → k` --- the continuation depends on the callee's result, the second `etf_combine` argument. `c → k` and `FunctionResult p → k` are combine dependencies of the analysis, kept visibly separate from `intra g`. They are never added to `intra g`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `cfg_succ_rel` | 28 | definition cfg_succ_rel :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "cfg_succ_rel g =<br>     {(u, v) \| u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>   ∪ {(c, ce) \| c ca ce k. (c, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}<br>   ∪ {(c, k) \| c ca ce k. (c, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}<br>   ∪ {(FunctionResult p, k) \| c ca p k. (c, ca, FunctionEntry p, k) ∈ calls g}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_succ` | 68 | definition cfg_succ :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_succ g u w ↔ (u, w) ∈ cfg_succ_rel g" |  |
| definition | `cfg_reaches` | 71 | definition cfg_reaches :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_reaches g v v0 ↔ (v, v0) ∈ (cfg_succ_rel g)^*" |  |
| definition | `cone` | 74 | definition cone :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "cone g v0 = {v. cfg_reaches g v v0}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_exit` | 158 | definition cfg_exit :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "cfg_exit g = (case cfg_entry g of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| n ⇒ n)" | Whole-program completion is the entry procedure's `FunctionResult`: the compiler sets `cfg_entry (compile_prog … mnm …) = FunctionEntry mnm`, so `cfg_exit` is `FunctionResult mnm`. |

### src/CFG/CFG_Transfer.thy

> `edge_step` (from `Voblint_CFG.CFG_Def`) is the primitive intra-edge semantics. `edge_collect` is its pointwise lift to store sets --- the successors of any source store in `S` --- so the abstract set transformer is derived from the concrete step and the two cannot drift. This is the per-`intra`-edge transfer the constraint system folds over.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `edge_collect` | 12 | definition edge_collect :: "edge_action ⇒ store set ⇒ store set" where<br>  "edge_collect a S = {t. ∃s∈S. edge_step a s = Some t}" | `edge_step` (from `Voblint_CFG.CFG_Def`) is the primitive intra-edge semantics. `edge_collect` is its pointwise lift to store sets --- the successors of any source store in `S` --- so the abstract set transformer is derived from the concrete step and the two cannot drift. This is the per-`intra`-edge transfer the constraint system folds over. |

### src/CFG/Collecting/CFG_Local_Trace.thy

> An `ltr` represents one procedure activation and its concrete ancestry. Its local path contains `(cfg_node, store)` pairs; structural constructors record the suspended caller and, after resumption, the completed callee. Activation contexts are projections of this structure rather than fields stored in the trace. Local flow follows `intra`, including `EA_Ret` into the matching `FunctionResult`. A `calls` tuple supplies the call site, callee entry, and continuation. The call rule builds the parameter-bound entry store with `call_enter`. The resume rule combines caller locals, callee globals, and the return value with `combine_collect`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `ltr` | 31 | datatype ltr =<br>    Root "(cfg_node * store) list"<br>  \| Call ltr "(cfg_node * store) list"<br>  \| Resume ltr ltr "(cfg_node * store) list" | • `Root p` --- the main activation, with local path `p`. • `Call caller p` --- a callee whose local path `p` starts at the callee-entry store; `caller` is the exact suspended caller, frozen at the call node. • `Resume current callee p` --- the activation continued past a completed call. `current` is that activation frozen at its call node (the value that spawned `callee`); `callee` is the retained completed callee subtree; `p` is the continued path. |
| primrec | `path` | 41 | primrec path :: "ltr ⇒ (cfg_node * store) list" where<br>  "path (Root p)       = p"<br>\| "path (Call _ p)     = p"<br>\| "path (Resume _ _ p) = p" | `path` is the activation-local control-flow path. `sink_node` and `sink_store` return its final program point and final store. |
| definition | `entry_store` | 46 | definition entry_store :: "ltr ⇒ store" where<br>  "entry_store t = snd (hd (path t))" |  |
| definition | `sink_node` | 49 | definition sink_node :: "ltr ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "sink_node t = fst (last (path t))" |  |
| definition | `sink_store` | 52 | definition sink_store :: "ltr ⇒ store" where<br>  "sink_store t = snd (last (path t))" |  |
| fun | `caller_of` | 59 | fun caller_of :: "ltr ⇒ ltr option" where<br>  "caller_of (Root _)             = None"<br>\| "caller_of (Call caller _)      = Some caller"<br>\| "caller_of (Resume current _ _) = caller_of current" | `caller_of` recovers the creating caller of an activation. It descends the frozen `caller` field of a `Resume`, so it works uniformly for a returned callee of any constructor --- this is what makes nested and recursive returns compose. |
| primrec | `extend` | 68 | primrec extend :: "ltr ⇒ (cfg_node * store) ⇒ ltr" where<br>  "extend (Root p) x       = Root (p @ [x])"<br>\| "extend (Call c p) x     = Call c (p @ [x])"<br>\| "extend (Resume c d p) x = Resume c d (p @ [x])" | `extend` appends one step to the innermost local path; it never touches an outer constructor's caller/callee fields. |
| fun | `key` | 82 | fun key :: "(cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ ltr ⇒ 'c" where<br>  "key enterc seedc (Root _)                  = seedc"<br>\| "key enterc seedc (Call parent p)           =<br>     enterc (sink_node parent) (key enterc seedc parent) (snd (hd p))"<br>\| "key enterc seedc (Resume current callee _) = key enterc seedc current" | The activation context is computed from the concrete trace after the fact and is activation-stable: fixed when the activation is created and unchanged by the calls it later makes and returns from. `Root` carries the seed; a `Call` routes the caller context on the call site and the callee-entry store --- `sink_node parent` is exactly the call site, since `extend` only ever appends to the callee path and leaves `parent` frozen there; a `Resume` keeps the resumed caller's own context, so a completed call does not repartition the caller. Exposing the call site to `enterc` is what makes a call-site-keyed context (a k-call-string) expressible as an instance rather than only as a generator-side hook. |
| inductive_set | `valid_ltr` | 102 | inductive_set valid_ltr :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ ltr set" for g S where<br>  init:<br>    "s ∈ S<br>     ⟹ Root [(cfg_entry g, s)] ∈ valid_ltr g S"<br>\| intra:<br>    "t ∈ valid_ltr g S<br>     ⟹ (sink_node t, a, v) ∈ intra g<br>     ⟹ edge_step a (sink_store t) = Some s'<br>     ⟹ extend t (v, s') ∈ valid_ltr g S"<br>\| call:<br>    "caller ∈ valid_ltr g S<br>     ⟹ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>     ⟹ Call caller [(FunctionEntry p, call_enter (CallEdge dst pars args) (sink_store caller))]<br>         ∈ valid_ltr g S"<br>\| ret:<br>... (19 more lines) | `valid_ltr` is the least set closed under four concrete operations: an initial main activation at `cfg_entry`; an `intra` step; a call; and a return. Each rule reads exactly the relation for its phenomenon. `intra` carries no side condition --- calls are not `intra` members, so they are untraversable by typing. `call` enters the callee named by the `calls` edge at the callee-entry store `enter_state`. `ret` matches the callee's `FunctionResult p` against the `FunctionEntry p` of a concrete `calls` edge leaving the caller's node, and resumes at the continuation stored in that same edge; the resumed state is `combine_collect`. There is no `combines` lookup and no scan for a compatible context. |
| definition | `call_enter_store` | 632 | definition call_enter_store :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ store ⇒ store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "call_enter_store g c s t ↔<br>     (∃dst pars args p cont. (c, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>        ∧ t = call_enter (CallEdge dst pars args) s)" |  |
| definition | `activation_collect` | 763 | definition activation_collect ::<br>  "(cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set" where<br>  "activation_collect enterc seedc g S v c =<br>     {sink_store t \| t. t ∈ valid_ltr g S ∧ sink_node t = v ∧ key enterc seedc t = c}" | The activation-sensitive collecting is the sink stores of valid traces reaching `v` whose activation context is `c`. |

### src/CFG/Collecting/LTR_Abstract.thy

> The smallest domain-free proof interface through which an abstract over-approximation soundly covers the stack-faithful semantics `valid_ltr`. The abstract object is a context-indexed concretization • `acc :: cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set` --- the set of stores the analysis admits at node `v` in abstract context `c`. No `sound_domain`, `abs_state`, solver, or DG dependency appears here. The interface fixes four closure obligations --- a root seed (`ROOT`), an intra edge (`EDGE`), a call routing (`CALL`), and a return combine (`COMB`) --- one per phenomenon, and proves that every valid trace's sink store lands in its own activation slot. The load-bearing clause is `COMB`: the callee is read at context `enterc c1 es` --- the caller context `c1` routed through the callee's own bound entry store --- so a return can only compose the callee whose context descends from the caller it resumes. The correlation is not an extra parameter: `callee_entry_invariant` makes \<open>enterc c1 es\<close> a theorem of `valid_ltr`, so a proof-level activation record is unnecessary. The abstract context type `'c` is arbitrary; `key` may quotient distinct activations to the same context. Nothing here claims a key is an exact activation identity --- only that each valid trace lands in some slot, matched at return.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| locale | `ltr_gamma` | 33 | locale ltr_gamma =<br>  fixes g :: cfg and S :: "store set"<br>    and acc :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set"<br>    and enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c"<br>    and seedc :: 'c<br>  assumes ROOT: "⋀s. s ∈ S ⟹ s ∈ acc (cfg_entry g) seedc"<br>    and EDGE: "⋀u a v c s s'. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g<br>        ⟹ s ∈ acc u c ⟹ edge_step a s = Some s' ⟹ s' ∈ acc v c"<br>    and CALL: "⋀u dst pars args p cont c s. (u, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>        ⟹ s ∈ acc u c ⟹ call_enter (CallEdge dst pars args) s<br>              ∈ acc (FunctionEntry p) (enterc u c (call_enter (CallEdge dst pars args) s))"<br>    and COMB: "⋀cl dst pars args p cont c1 s t es.<br>        (cl, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>        ⟹ s ∈ acc cl c1 ⟹ t ∈ acc (FunctionResult p) (enterc cl c1 es)<br>        ⟹ call_enter_store g cl s es<br>... (2 more lines) |  |
| abbreviation | `bnd` | 53 | abbreviation bnd :: "ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "bnd u ≡ sink_store u ∈ acc (sink_node u) (key enterc seedc u)" | `bnd u`: the sink store of `u` is admitted at `u`'s own node and activation context. |
| definition | `gamma_ltr` | 56 | definition gamma_ltr :: "ltr set" where<br>  "gamma_ltr = {t. bnd t}" |  |

### src/CFG/Collecting/LTR_Collect.thy

> The stack-faithful concrete carrier of a procedure-aware CFG is the local-trace set `valid_ltr`. This theory presents it as a least fixed point of an explicit monotone set transformer `ltr_F`, defines the forgetful projection of a trace set to a CFG node (`ltr_collect`, the concrete collector), and proves the collecting equations, the context-sensitive/insensitive bridge to `activation_collect`. `ltr_F` has exactly the four clauses of `valid_ltr` (root, intra step, call, return), each reading exactly the relation for its phenomenon: `intra` for local extension, `calls` for entering a callee and for recovering a continuation. The return clause mirrors `valid_ltr.ret` literally --- it recovers the caller from the completed callee's own ancestry through `caller_of`, never by an independent choice, and matches the callee's `FunctionResult p` against a concrete `calls` edge. There is no global exit node: whole-program completion is collection at `FunctionResult main`, and a procedure result is an ordinary collected node, not a separate summary mechanism. No solver, DG, or abstract-domain theory is imported.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ltr_F` | 28 | definition ltr_F :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ ltr set ⇒ ltr set" where<br>  "ltr_F g S T =<br>      {Root [(cfg_entry g, s)] \| s. s ∈ S}<br>    ∪ {extend t (v, s') \| t a v s'.<br>          t ∈ T ∧ (sink_node t, a, v) ∈ intra g ∧ edge_step a (sink_store t) = Some s'}<br>    ∪ {Call caller [(FunctionEntry p, call_enter (CallEdge dst pars args) (sink_store caller))]<br>          \| caller dst pars args p cont.<br>          caller ∈ T ∧ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g}<br>    ∪ {Resume caller callee<br>          (path caller @ [(cont, combine_collect dst (sink_store caller) (sink_store callee))])<br>          \| callee caller p dst pars args cont.<br>          callee ∈ T ∧ caller_of callee = Some caller<br>          ∧ sink_node callee = FunctionResult p<br>          ∧ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g}" |  |
| definition | `ltr_collect` | 115 | definition ltr_collect :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ store set" where<br>  "ltr_collect g S v =<br>     {sink_store t \| t. t ∈ valid_ltr g S ∧ sink_node t = v}" | `ltr_collect` is the concrete collecting view: the sink stores of valid traces reaching node `v`. `activation_collect` (`CFG_Local_Trace`) is the context-indexed collector, filtering those by the structural activation key; the key type is not required finite, so a keyed bucket is not claimed to be an exact activation identity. |
| definition | `collect_result` | 121 | definition collect_result :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ pname ⇒ store set" where<br>  "collect_result g S p = ltr_collect g S (FunctionResult p)" | `collect_result` is a convenience view --- collection at a procedure result --- not a distinct mechanism. Whole-program completion is `collect_result g S main`. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Compile_Invariants.thy

> The compiler preserves procedure boundaries, separates call edges from ordinary control flow, and allocates disjoint statement ranges. These properties connect source commands to activation-local traces without exposing compiler counters to semantic clients.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `wf_compile_input` | 21 | definition wf_compile_input ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "wf_compile_input Π ps mnm main ↔<br>     distinct ps ∧<br>     set ps = {p. Π p ≠ None} - {mnm} ∧<br>     mnm ∉ set ps ∧<br>     wf_source_program Π mnm main" | `mnm` names the distinguished entry procedure, declared in `Π` with an empty formal list and body `main`. The callee list `ps` enumerates the other declared procedures (`dom Π` minus `mnm`), so `FunctionEntry mnm` / `FunctionResult mnm` never collide with a callee's nodes, yet `FunctionEntry mnm` is an ordinary `proc_activation Π mnm main` activation. |
| fun | `returns_in` | 57 | fun returns_in :: "aexp option ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "returns_in e (Return e') = (e = e')"<br>\| "returns_in e (Seq c1 c2) = (returns_in e c1 ∨ returns_in e c2)"<br>\| "returns_in e (If _ c1 c2) = (returns_in e c1 ∨ returns_in e c2)"<br>\| "returns_in e (While _ c) = returns_in e c"<br>\| "returns_in e _ = False" |  |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Compile_Locality.thy

> An activation's own local `path` stays inside one compiled procedure fragment. A *fragment* of procedure `r` compiled over `[m, m')` is `FunctionEntry r`, `FunctionResult r`, and the `Statement` nodes in that counter range. Every intra edge keeps both endpoints in one fragment, every call edge keeps its source and continuation there (the callee `FunctionEntry` is the only cross-fragment endpoint), and distinct fragments are node-disjoint, so a single activation entered at `FunctionEntry p` reaches `FunctionResult q` only for `p = q`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `pfn` | 17 | definition pfn :: "pname ⇒ nat ⇒ nat ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "pfn r m m' = insert (FunctionEntry r) (insert (FunctionResult r) (Statement ` {m..&lt;m'}))" | An activation's own local `path` stays inside one compiled procedure fragment. A *fragment* of procedure `r` compiled over `[m, m')` is `FunctionEntry r`, `FunctionResult r`, and the `Statement` nodes in that counter range. Every intra edge keeps both endpoints in one fragment, every call edge keeps its source and continuation there (the callee `FunctionEntry` is the only cross-fragment endpoint), and distinct fragments are node-disjoint, so a single activation entered at `FunctionEntry p` reaches `FunctionResult q` only for `p = q`. |
| definition | `frag_ok` | 1337 | definition frag_ok :: "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "frag_ok Π ps mnm main u ↔<br>     (∃r d m m' Ep Kp. Π r = Some d<br>        ∧ compile_proc Π r d m = (m', Ep, Kp)<br>        ∧ (FunctionEntry r, EA_Nop, Statement m) ∈ intra (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)<br>        ∧ fst (hd (path u)) = FunctionEntry r<br>        ∧ (∀nd ∈ fst ` set (path u). nd ∈ pfn r m m'))<br>   ∨ (∃p s. path u = [(FunctionEntry p, s)] ∧ Π p = None)" | `frag_ok u`: the activation `u` either lies wholly inside a genuine procedure fragment (declared body, entry wiring, all path nodes in the fragment) or is a single-node activation stuck at an undeclared `FunctionEntry` --- which can never advance and never reaches a `FunctionResult`. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Control_Residual.thy

> `control_at Π p c k n residual v` locates a runtime residual at node `v` inside the fragment compiled from command `c` of procedure `p` at base counter `n` with continuation `k`. The relation describes control only; the simulation states store agreement separately. The continuation is the node a completed fragment occupies. A command that has run to `SKIP` is therefore located at `k` itself rather than at a private exit node, which is why the `Done` rules below name no fragment node. A call crosses procedure fragments. The caller fragment records its call site and continuation, while the callee fragment locates the callee body. The activation stack of `cstep` connects both locations.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `control_at` | 41 | inductive control_at ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool"<br>  for Π :: proc_table and p :: pname<br>where<br>  Skip:<br>    "control_at Π p SKIP k n SKIP (Statement n)"<br>\| Assign:<br>    "control_at Π p (Assign x a) k n (Assign x a) (Statement n)"<br>\| AssignDone:<br>    "control_at Π p (Assign x a) k n SKIP k"<br>\| SeqLeft:<br>    "control_at Π p c1 (Statement (n + csize c1)) n r v ⟹<br>     control_at Π p (Seq c1 c2) k n (Seq r c2) v"<br>\| SeqRight:<br>    "falls_through c1 ⟹<br>... (29 more lines) | Residual coverage. For each source form the located clauses fix which node the residual occupies; runtime-only forms are handled as follows. • `SKIP`, assignment, sequence (before and after left completion), conditionals, loops, call site, entered continuation, and explicit `Return` all map to a compiled node or to the continuation. • `Restore` and `Unwind` are runtime-only activation markers, not located here: once a `Return` fires the CFG control is already at `FunctionResult p` (through the `EA_Ret` edge) and the remaining activation return is discharged by the located executor `cstep`, not by a fragment node. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Control_Simulation.thy

> The compiled execution `cstep` simulates the source small-step `pstep`. Both run an activation stack: a source frame carries the caller store and destination, a `cframe` additionally records the CFG continuation node. The simulation relation is direct --- literal store equality, `control_at` locating the active residual at the CFG node, and `frames_match` tying the two stacks one-for-one. This layer proves the static *compiler-location* facts: a located residual whose head is a base command emits exactly the CFG edge the compiler generated for it, and the head's source step re-locates the successor residual. The intra, call, and return step simulations are built on these.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `intra_step` | 408 | inductive intra_step ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ com × store × frame list ⇒ com × store × frame list ⇒ bool" for Π where<br>  IAssign: "intra_step Π (Assign x a, s, frs) (SKIP, s(x := aval a s), frs)"<br>\| ISeq1:   "intra_step Π (Seq SKIP c2, s, frs) (c2, s, frs)"<br>\| ISeq2:   "intra_step Π (c1, s, frs) (c1', s', frs) ⟹<br>            intra_step Π (Seq c1 c2, s, frs) (Seq c1' c2, s', frs)"<br>\| IIfTrue: "bval b s ⟹ intra_step Π (If b c1 c2, s, frs) (c1, s, frs)"<br>\| IIfFalse:"¬ bval b s ⟹ intra_step Π (If b c1 c2, s, frs) (c2, s, frs)"<br>\| IWhile:  "intra_step Π (While b c, s, frs) (If b (Seq c (While b c)) SKIP, s, frs)"<br><br>inductive_cases intra_SkipE:   "intra_step Π (SKIP, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_AssignE: "intra_step Π (Assign x a, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_SeqE:    "intra_step Π (Seq c1 c2, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_IfE:     "intra_step Π (If b c1 c2, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_WhileE:  "intra_step Π (While b c, s, frs) y"<br>... (2 more lines) | `intra_step` is the fragment of `pstep` that stays inside one activation without initiating a return: assignment, sequencing (head execution and head completion), both conditionals, and the loop unfolding. It excludes `Call` (pushes a frame), `Return` (produces `Unwind`), and the runtime-only `Restore` / `Unwind` steps. Every intra step preserves the frame stack and keeps the residual source-shaped. |
| definition | `ret_store` | 704 | definition ret_store :: "aexp option ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "ret_store e s = s(ret_var := (case e of None ⇒ s ret_var \| Some a ⇒ aval a s))" | The store published by a return: the callee's `ret_var` is set to the evaluated return value (`Some e`) or left untouched (`None`). It is the common store reached by both the source `Return` step and the compiled `EA_Ret` edge. |
| definition | `proc_activation` | 739 | definition proc_activation :: "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "proc_activation Π p c0 ↔ (∃decl. Π p = Some decl ∧ c0 = body decl)" | `proc_activation Π p c0` pins an activation's fragment origin `c0` to a genuine procedure body: `p` is declared in `Π` and `c0` is exactly its body. `csim` carries it on every activation so that preservation is stated only over real procedures --- `control_at`'s fragment parameter `c0` is otherwise free and would admit spurious (non-procedure) activations for which ordinary fall-through preservation is false. This is the *only* thing added to `csim`; all compile tuples, edge inclusions, and graph wiring stay in `procs_compiled`. |
| fun | `seq_after` | 762 | fun seq_after :: "com ⇒ com list ⇒ com" where<br>  "seq_after c [] = c"<br>\| "seq_after c (a # as) = seq_after (Seq c a) as" | A call site's caller has, after the call, a finite left-spine of residual continuations `afters`: the source commands sequenced after the call, in execution order, collected while descending the current activation's left-associated `Seq` spine. `seq_after c afters` wraps them around the active head `c`, appending each in order: `seq_after c [s3, s4] = Seq (Seq c s3) s4`. A *tail-position* call (the call is the caller's last command, including the top-level entry call) has no continuation --- `afters = []` --- and the caller resumes at bare `c`; a call with pending commands carries them in `afters` and the caller resumes at `seq_after SKIP afters`. Every case runs through the same activation machinery (the frame is pushed and popped identically); only the caller's leftover source differs. The continuation list is orthogonal to `csim`'s recursive nesting: `afters` holds pending commands *inside one activation*, while recursive nesting holds suspended *caller activations*. |
| fun | `unwinding` | 1016 | fun unwinding :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "unwinding Unwind = True"<br>\| "unwinding (Seq u c2) = (unwinding u ∧ c2 ≠ Restore)"<br>\| "unwinding _ = False" | Once a callee finishes, the CFG is already at `FunctionResult p` while the source propagates toward the frame-pop rule. `unwinding` describes an `Unwind` travelling up through the callee's residual, dropping the dead code it skips (never the activation's own `Restore`); `pop_ready` describes the whole activation body in that phase --- either the normal fall-through `Restore` or an `Unwind` spine `Seq u Restore` from an explicit `Return`. Both pop the single caller frame with one `cstep` return; `unwinding` propagation is matched by *no* CFG step (the CFG waits at `FunctionResult p`). |
| fun | `pop_ready` | 1021 | fun pop_ready :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "pop_ready Restore = True"<br>\| "pop_ready (Seq u Restore) = unwinding u"<br>\| "pop_ready _ = False" |  |
| definition | `compiled_at` | 1051 | definition compiled_at ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat ⇒ bool" where<br>  "compiled_at Π g p c0 k n ↔<br>     (∃n' en E K. compile Π p c0 k n = (n', en, E, K)<br>        ∧ E ⊆ intra g ∧ K ⊆ calls g<br>        ∧ (falls_through c0 ⟶ (k, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) ∈ intra g))" | `compiled_at Pi g p c0 k n` witnesses that the fragment `c0`, compiled at the exact offset `n` with continuation `k`, is embedded in the target graph `g`: its intra edges lie in `intra g` and its call edges in `calls g`. `csim` carries it on every activation at the *same* `k` and `n` that `control_at` uses, so the located node and the fragment's edges are genuinely present in `g`. Compilation is shift-parametric --- neither the offset nor the continuation is fixed by the syntax --- so graph membership at the chosen offset is the missing fact, and this predicate supplies it. The continuation of a procedure body is its epilogue node, which is why the fall-through return edge is stated from `k`. That edge exists only when the body can actually fall through to it, so the requirement is guarded by `falls_through`; `control_at_SKIP_imp_falls_through` discharges the guard wherever a completed (`SKIP`) residual needs the edge. |
| inductive | `csim` | 1117 | inductive csim :: "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ com × store × frame list<br>                    ⇒ cfg_node × store × cframe list ⇒ bool" for Π g where<br>  Base:<br>    "control_at Π p c0 k n c v ⟹ compiled_at Π g p c0 k n ⟹ proc_activation Π p c0 ⟹<br>     csim Π g (c, s, []) (v, s, [])"<br>\| Nested:<br>    "csim Π g (inner, s, frs) (v, s, stk) ⟹<br>     control_at Π pc c0c kc nc (seq_after SKIP afters) cont ⟹<br>     compiled_at Π g pc c0c kc nc ⟹<br>     proc_activation Π pc c0c ⟹<br>     csim Π g (seq_after (Seq inner Restore) afters, s, frs @ [Frame caller dst])<br>              (v, s, stk @ [(cont, dst, caller)])"<br>\| Returning:<br>    "pop_ready w ⟹<br>     control_at Π pc c0c kc nc (seq_after SKIP afters) cont ⟹<br>... (6 more lines) | `csim` relates a source configuration `(c, s, frs)` to a CFG configuration `(v, s, stk)` with literal store equality (the same `s`). It bridges the two continuation representations: the source keeps every suspended caller's continuation nested inside the single command `c` (defunctionalized as `Seq (Seq inner Restore) after` wrappings, one per activation), while the CFG keeps them as continuation nodes on the frame stack. The nesting runs outermost-first: after `main` calls `f` calls `g`, the command is `Seq (Seq (Seq (Seq C Restore) B) Restore) A` with `A` the outermost (main's) continuation and `C` the active (g's) residual. So the *top* command layer pairs with the *last* (outermost) frame, and the active node/store thread unchanged down to the `Base` case. • `Base` --- one activation: the active residual `c` is located at `v` and the frame stacks are empty. • `Nested` --- peel the outermost caller: the command's top layer `Seq (Seq inner Restore) after` and the last frame `Frame caller dst` pair with the CFG's last frame `(cont, dst, caller)`; the caller's post-call residual `Seq SKIP after` is located at the continuation node `cont`; and the remaining inner structure is related recursively. `csim` has three constructors --- one per source phase. `Base` and `Nested` cover *ordinary* execution (residuals located by `control_at` at `Statement` nodes). `Returning` covers the *return-in-progress* phase: once a callee's body has completed (fall-through), the innermost source layer is `Seq Restore after` and the CFG sits at `FunctionResult p`, one `cstep` return away from resuming the caller. Like `Base` it is an innermost constructor (an alternative to it), wrapped by any number of outer `Nested` layers. It carries exactly the *one* frame the return pops (the immediate caller's save); the outer callers' frames are consumed by the wrapping `Nested` layers. Its premise locates the *resumed caller* --- the ordinary residual `Seq SKIP after` the return will land in, at the continuation node `cont` --- so return completion pops the single frame and the caller resumes as a `Base` activation (the outer `Nested` wrapping is untouched). The active store is a free rider (`control_at` is store-independent). |
| fun | `is_returning` | 1212 | fun is_returning :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_returning Restore = True"<br>\| "is_returning Unwind = True"<br>\| "is_returning (Seq c1 c2) = is_returning c1"<br>\| "is_returning _ = False" | `is_returning c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is `Restore` or `Unwind`: the shape of every returning-phase command --- normal fall-through or an explicit-return `Unwind` spine, ordinary or `Nested`-wrapped. |
| fun | `head_call` | 1230 | fun head_call :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "head_call (Call dst q actuals) = True"<br>\| "head_call (Seq c1 c2) = head_call c1"<br>\| "head_call _ = False" | `head_call c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is a `Call`: the shape of an active residual whose next `pstep` fires the call. Like `is_returning` it descends the leftmost `Seq` spine and so is insensitive to `seq_after` wrapping and to whether the activation is a `Base` or a `Nested` inner. |
| fun | `head_return` | 1272 | fun head_return :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "head_return (Return e) = True"<br>\| "head_return (Seq c1 c2) = head_return c1"<br>\| "head_return _ = False" | `head_return c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is a source `Return`: the shape whose next `pstep` initiates a return (`Return` `-->` `Unwind`). Like the other head classifiers it descends the leftmost `Seq` spine. |
| fun | `ret_guarded` | 1311 | fun ret_guarded :: "bool ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ret_guarded rok SKIP = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Assign x a) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Seq c1 c2) =<br>     (if c2 = Restore then ret_guarded True c1<br>      else ret_guarded rok c1 ∧ ret_guarded rok c2)"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (If b c1 c2) = (ret_guarded rok c1 ∧ ret_guarded rok c2)"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (While b c) = ret_guarded rok c"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Call dst p actuals) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Return e) = rok"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok Restore = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok Unwind = rok" | `ret_guarded rok c` is the deep source-only well-formedness check: an explicit `Return` (or an in-flight `Unwind`) is admissible only where `rok` holds --- inside a called activation. Permission is *granted by the activation tail*: the right child `Restore` of a `Seq` marks a callee body, so its left child is checked with `rok = True`, whereas ordinary continuations inherit the ambient `rok`. A bare `Restore` (the transient tail after the callee body reduces to `SKIP`) is always admissible. Crucially this looks *behind* `Restore` boundaries: a `Return` hidden in a base-activation continuation is rejected, which a head-only check misses --- exactly the configuration a frame pop would later surface at the base. |
| definition | `source_wf` | 1338 | definition source_wf :: "com × store × frame list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "source_wf cfg ↔ (case cfg of (c, s, frs) ⇒ ret_guarded False c)" | The runtime invariant: the active command is `ret_guarded` at the base activation (returns forbidden until a call opens one). Unlike a head-only guard this is preserved by `pstep`, because it already forbids the base-activation returns that a frame pop would expose. |
| definition | `procs_compiled` | 1757 | definition procs_compiled :: "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "procs_compiled Π g ↔<br>     (∀p decl. Π p = Some decl ⟶<br>        (∃k n n' en E K.<br>           compile Π p (body decl) k n = (n', en, E, K)<br>         ∧ E ⊆ intra g ∧ K ⊆ calls g<br>         ∧ (FunctionEntry p, EA_Nop, en) ∈ intra g<br>         ∧ (falls_through (body decl) ⟶<br>              (k, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) ∈ intra g)<br>         ∧ source_com (body decl)))" | `procs_compiled Π g` is the static compiler-correctness certificate for the target graph: every declared procedure's body is compiled into `g`, with its intra/call edges included and the entry/exit wiring present (`FunctionEntry p` `--EA_Nop-->` body entry, and body exit `--EA_Ret-->` `FunctionResult p`). It is kept *separate* from `csim` (which describes only the dynamic configuration correspondence): the returning phase needs none of it, while the ordinary and call phases receive it as a single static premise and read off the concrete facts through the projection lemmas below. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Located_Exec.thy

> `cstep` is the concrete execution of the two-relation procedure-aware CFG, in the activation-stack shape that mirrors the source `pstep` and drives the located simulation. A `cconf` pairs the current node with a store and a stack of pending activations; each `cframe` records the continuation node, the caller destination, and the saved caller store --- exactly the payload the resume transfer `combine_collect` needs. There are three transitions, one per graph phenomenon: • intra flow follows an `intra` edge and applies `edge_step` (covering `EA_Nop`, assignment, both assume forms, and `EA_Ret` into `FunctionResult`); the stack is unchanged; • a call follows a `calls` edge, applies the caller-side `call_enter`, moves to the callee `FunctionEntry`, and pushes one activation carrying the continuation; • a return/resume fires at `FunctionResult`, pops the top activation, and lands at its recorded continuation with the combined store `combine_collect`. A return does not use a `FunctionResult p --> cont` intra edge. The activation stack supplies the continuation, so one `FunctionResult` node serves every caller and the stack contains one entry per call.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `cframe` | 29 | type_synonym cframe = "cfg_node × vname option × store" | `cstep` is the concrete execution of the two-relation procedure-aware CFG, in the activation-stack shape that mirrors the source `pstep` and drives the located simulation. A `cconf` pairs the current node with a store and a stack of pending activations; each `cframe` records the continuation node, the caller destination, and the saved caller store --- exactly the payload the resume transfer `combine_collect` needs. There are three transitions, one per graph phenomenon: • intra flow follows an `intra` edge and applies `edge_step` (covering `EA_Nop`, assignment, both assume forms, and `EA_Ret` into `FunctionResult`); the stack is unchanged; • a call follows a `calls` edge, applies the caller-side `call_enter`, moves to the callee `FunctionEntry`, and pushes one activation carrying the continuation; • a return/resume fires at `FunctionResult`, pops the top activation, and lands at its recorded continuation with the combined store `combine_collect`. A return does not use a `FunctionResult p --> cont` intra edge. The activation stack supplies the continuation, so one `FunctionResult` node serves every caller and the stack contains one entry per call. |
| type_synonym | `cconf` | 30 | type_synonym cconf = "cfg_node × store × cframe list" |  |
| inductive | `cstep` | 32 | inductive cstep :: "cfg ⇒ cconf ⇒ cconf ⇒ bool" for g where<br>  Intra:<br>    "(u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟹ edge_step a s = Some s' ⟹<br>     cstep g (u, s, stk) (v, s', stk)"<br>\| Call:<br>    "(u, CallEdge dst pars actuals, FunctionEntry q, cont) ∈ calls g ⟹<br>     cstep g (u, s, stk)<br>       (FunctionEntry q, call_enter (CallEdge dst pars actuals) s, (cont, dst, s) # stk)"<br>\| Return:<br>    "cstep g (FunctionResult q, t, (cont, dst, caller) # stk)<br>       (cont, combine_collect dst caller t, stk)" |  |
| fun | `act_frames` | 86 | fun act_frames :: "frame list ⇒ (store × vname option) list" where<br>  "act_frames [] = []"<br>\| "act_frames (Frame s d # frs) = (s, d) # act_frames frs" | Both stacks record procedure activations. `act_frames` removes the CFG-only continuation component and retains the caller store and destination used by the source. |
| definition | `cframe_act` | 90 | definition cframe_act :: "cframe ⇒ store × vname option" where<br>  "cframe_act cf = (case cf of (cont, d, s) ⇒ (s, d))" |  |
| definition | `frames_match` | 96 | definition frames_match :: "frame list ⇒ cframe list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "frames_match frs stk = (act_frames frs = map cframe_act stk)" | `frames_match` ties a source frame stack to a CFG activation stack: the activation frames agree, in order, on caller store and destination (the continuation node is the CFG's own bookkeeping and is not visible to the source). |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Located_LTR.thy

> The stack-faithful bridge from compiled source execution to the canonical activation-local semantics `valid_ltr`. A CFG-located configuration `cconf` (advanced one edge at a time by `cstep`) is related to a valid activation-local trace: the current activation is the trace, and the runtime `cframe` stack is its `caller_of` chain. Each `cstep` rule maps one-to-one onto a `valid_ltr` constructor (`intra` / `call` / `ret`), so a source run --- simulated into a `cstep` run by `csim` --- extends the accumulated trace.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `stack_repr` | 29 | inductive stack_repr :: "cfg ⇒ cframe list ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" for g where<br>  empty: "caller_of t = None ⟹ stack_repr g [] t"<br>\| frame: "caller_of t = Some c ⟹ sink_store c = caller<br>          ⟹ fst (hd (path t)) = FunctionEntry p<br>          ⟹ (sink_node c, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>          ⟹ stack_repr g stk c<br>          ⟹ stack_repr g ((cont, dst, caller) # stk) t" | `stack_repr` walks `caller_of` in lockstep with the runtime frame list: each `cframe` `(cont, dst, caller)` pins the caller activation `c` --- its frozen store `sink_store c = caller` --- and records the concrete `calls` edge that spawned the child, whose callee `FunctionEntry p` equals the child's (path-invariant) entry node. |
| definition | `ltr_repr` | 39 | definition ltr_repr :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cconf ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ltr_repr g S cf t = (case cf of (v, s, stk) ⇒<br>     t ∈ valid_ltr g S ∧ sink_node t = v ∧ sink_store t = s ∧ stack_repr g stk t)" | `ltr_repr` pins a valid trace to a located configuration: the trace's sink is the current node/store, and its caller chain is the runtime stack. |
| definition | `located_ltr` | 43 | definition located_ltr :: "cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cconf ⇒ bool" where<br>  "located_ltr g S cf = (∃t. ltr_repr g S cf t)" |  |

### src/CFG/VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy

> Compile a `com` program directly into the two-relation procedure-aware CFG. Nodes are `Statement n` (allocated from a counter), `FunctionEntry p`, and `FunctionResult p`. The compiler threads the enclosing procedure name `p`, so a source `Return` and the normal fall-through both reach `FunctionResult p` through an `EA_Ret` intra edge. Compilation is continuation passing: the node reached when a fragment falls through is an input, not a result. A node therefore denotes a program point between transfer functions, and no fragment invents a node to hold a control flow that its own source construct does not have. Layout of one procedure `p` with body `b`: • `FunctionEntry p --EA_Nop--> entry(b)`; • the compiled body edges, with the epilogue node as the body's continuation; • `epilogue --EA_Ret None p--> FunctionResult p` (normal fall-through returns without a value); • each source `Return e` inside `b` compiles to `--EA_Ret e p--> FunctionResult p` and ignores its continuation, so an early return leaves no node behind. Control effects and their source counterparts: • source lexical restoration (`Scope` / `Restore`) becomes ordinary intra flow; `Scope` is represented by transparent `EA_Nop` brackets; • source `Return` / `Unwind` become an explicit `EA_Ret` edge into `FunctionResult`; • source activation handling becomes `FunctionResult` + the `calls`-edge continuation. Calls are emitted only into `calls`; a call never appears as an executable intra edge. `Restore` / `Unwind` are runtime-only (excluded by `source_com`); their compile clauses are trivial one-node stubs that never occur for a source program.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `csize` | 43 | fun csize :: "com ⇒ nat" where<br>  "csize SKIP = 1"<br>\| "csize (Assign x a) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Seq c1 c2) = csize c1 + csize c2"<br>\| "csize (If b c1 c2) = 1 + csize c1 + csize c2"<br>\| "csize (While b c) = 1 + csize c"<br>\| "csize (Call dst q actuals) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Return e) = 1"<br>\| "csize Restore = 1"<br>\| "csize Unwind = 1" | `csize c` is the number of `Statement` indices the fragment of `c` allocates, fixed by the source syntax alone. `compile` needs it before it recurses: `Seq` names the entry node of `c2` --- the continuation it hands to `c1` --- and that node is `Statement (n + csize c1)`. `compile_next_id` below ties the two together, so the two definitions cannot drift apart silently. |
| fun | `falls_through` | 66 | fun falls_through :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "falls_through SKIP = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Assign x a) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Seq c1 c2) = (falls_through c1 ∧ falls_through c2)"<br>\| "falls_through (If b c1 c2) = (falls_through c1 ∨ falls_through c2)"<br>\| "falls_through (While b c) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Call dst q actuals) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Return e) = False"<br>\| "falls_through Restore = True"<br>\| "falls_through Unwind = True" | `falls_through c`: control can leave the fragment of `c` through its continuation, rather than only through an explicit `Return`. It is a syntactic over-approximation of the dynamic property --- `While` is counted as falling through because its guard may fail on the first test, and a branch that is never taken still contributes. The two directions `compile_reaches_falls_through` and `compile_reaches_returns` turn it into the reachability facts the procedure wrapper needs, and `compile_proc` uses it to decide whether the epilogue node is worth allocating. |
| fun | `compile` | 104 | fun compile ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × cfg_node<br>        × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile Π p SKIP k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Nop, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Assign x a) k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Assign x a, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Seq c1 c2) k n =<br>     (let (n1, en1, E1, K1) = compile Π p c1 (Statement (n + csize c1)) n;<br>          (n2, en2, E2, K2) = compile Π p c2 k (n + csize c1)<br>      in (n2, en1, E1 ∪ E2, K1 ∪ K2))"<br>\| "compile Π p (If b c1 c2) k n =<br>... (22 more lines) | `compile Π p c k n` returns `(n', entry, intra_edges, call_edges)`, allocating `Statement` indices in the counter range `[n, n')`. The continuation `k` is an *input*: it is the node normal execution reaches when the fragment falls through. A fragment therefore never reports an exit, and a command that cannot fall through simply ignores `k`. Every fragment enters at `Statement n`, so the entry is determined by the counter and is reported only for convenience. The procedure table `Π` is threaded so a `Call` can label its `CallEdge` with the callee's declared formals; every other clause ignores it. Consequences of handing the continuation down rather than reporting an exit: • `Seq` needs no connecting `EA_Nop`: `c1`'s continuation *is* `c2`'s entry; • `If` allocates no merge node: both branches receive the same `k`, and the join happens at `k`, which is the program point after the conditional; • `While` needs no back-edge node: the body's continuation is the loop head; • `Return` ignores `k` and emits only its `EA_Ret` edge, so no unreachable continuation node is invented; • `Call` resumes at `k`, so the resume point is the following program point. `Restore` and `Unwind` are runtime-only (excluded by `source_com`), so their clauses are never exercised for a source program. They stay transparent: one node and a `EA_Nop` to the continuation, which is what a fragment whose entry and exit were the same node meant before the continuation became an input. Making them dead ends instead would cost the unconditional form of `compile_prog_entry_cfg_reaches_exit` for no gain, since no source program reaches these clauses. |
| definition | `compile_proc` | 166 | definition compile_proc ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ proc_decl ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile_proc Π p decl n =<br>     (let r = n + csize (body decl);<br>          (n', ben, E, K) = compile Π p (body decl) (Statement r) n<br>      in (Suc r,<br>          insert (FunctionEntry p, EA_Nop, ben)<br>            (if falls_through (body decl)<br>             then insert (Statement r, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) E<br>             else E),<br>          K))" | A procedure wraps its body between `FunctionEntry p` and `FunctionResult p`. The body is compiled with the *epilogue* node `Statement r` as its continuation, and the epilogue carries the implicit `EA_Ret None p`: normal fall-through returns without a value. A value is published only by an explicit source `Return e`, which compiles to its own `EA_Ret (Some e) p` edge inside the body. The epilogue exists so that every edge into `FunctionResult p` is a return edge. Passing `FunctionResult p` directly as the body continuation would let a trailing assignment target the result node, losing `compile_result_target`. It is allocated *after* the body, so statement indices stay in source order and `r` lies inside the procedure's own counter range --- which is what keeps edge locality a statement about one fragment. The epilogue is wired only when the body can reach it, that is when `falls_through` holds. A body all of whose paths end in an explicit `Return` leaves `Statement r` with no edges at all, so it is not a node of the compiled graph --- nodes exist extensionally, through the edges that mention them. `compile_reaches_returns` supplies the reachability that the epilogue edge would otherwise have carried. The counter still advances past `r` in both cases. Reserving the index costs one `nat` and keeps every `csize` equation, fragment range and procedure interval independent of `falls_through`. |
| fun | `compile_procs` | 182 | fun compile_procs ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile_procs Π [] n = (n, {}, {})"<br>\| "compile_procs Π (p # ps) n =<br>     (case Π p of<br>        None ⇒ compile_procs Π ps n<br>      \| Some decl ⇒<br>          (let (n1, E, K) = compile_proc Π p decl n;<br>               (n2, E', K') = compile_procs Π ps n1<br>           in (n2, E ∪ E', K ∪ K')))" |  |
| definition | `compile_prog` | 201 | definition compile_prog ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg"<br>where<br>  "compile_prog Π ps mnm main =<br>     (let (n1, Eprocs, Kprocs) = compile_procs Π ps 0;<br>          (n2, Emain, Kmain) = compile_proc Π mnm (proc_decl_of [] main) n1<br>      in ⦇ intra = Eprocs ∪ Emain, calls = Kprocs ∪ Kmain, cfg_entry = FunctionEntry mnm ⦈)" | The whole program: every declared procedure, plus `main` compiled under the designated main name `mnm` (which the well-formedness predicate keeps disjoint from `ps`). There is no global exit: whole-program completion is `FunctionResult mnm`, and the entry is `FunctionEntry mnm`. |
| definition | `frag_stmts` | 342 | definition frag_stmts ::<br>  "(cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>   ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set ⇒ nat set"<br>where<br>  "frag_stmts E K =<br>     {j. ∃a v. (Statement j, a, v) ∈ E} ∪ {j. ∃u a. (u, a, Statement j) ∈ E}<br>   ∪ {j. ∃act ce af. (Statement j, act, ce, af) ∈ K}<br>   ∪ {j. ∃u act ce. (u, act, ce, Statement j) ∈ K}" | The `Statement` indices a compiled fragment touches. Sources are always freshly allocated, so they lie in the fragment's own counter range; a target may in addition be the continuation, which belongs to the enclosing construct. `kstmt` names that one extra index, and is empty when the continuation is not a `Statement` node. |
| fun | `kstmt` | 351 | fun kstmt :: "cfg_node ⇒ nat set" where<br>  "kstmt (Statement j) = {j}"<br>\| "kstmt (FunctionEntry q) = {}"<br>\| "kstmt (FunctionResult q) = {}" |  |

## Examples

### src/Examples/CFG/Example_Compile_Baseline.thy

> Executable diagnostics for the compiled CFG, recorded before any compiler change. The structural successor relation `cfg_succ_rel` is set-valued; `succ_list` is its list view, with one clause per source of that relation: INTRA, ENTRY, COMB_CALLER, and COMB_RESULT.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `succ_list` | 21 | definition succ_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "succ_list g u =<br>     map (λ(s, a, v). v) (filter (λ(s, a, v). s = u) (cfg_intra_list g))<br>   @ concat<br>       (map (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>               (if c = u then [ce, k] else [])<br>             @ (case ce of<br>                  FunctionEntry q ⇒ (if u = FunctionResult q then [k] else [])<br>                \| _ ⇒ []))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `all_nodes_list` | 37 | definition all_nodes_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "all_nodes_list g =<br>     remdups<br>       (concat (map (λ(u, a, v). [u, v]) (cfg_intra_list g))<br>      @ concat (map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). [c, ce, k]) (cfg_calls_list g))<br>      @ [cfg_entry g])" | Every node mentioned by the graph, deduplicated: intra endpoints, call sites, callee entries, call continuations, and the graph entry. |
| definition | `entry_roots` | 47 | definition entry_roots :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "entry_roots g =<br>     filter (λv. case v of FunctionEntry q ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False) (all_nodes_list g)" | Roots for pruning: every procedure entry node in the graph, so that a procedure never called from `main` still counts as reachable rather than as compiler debris. |
| definition | `reach_step` | 51 | definition reach_step :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_step g vs = remdups (vs @ concat (map (succ_list g) vs))" |  |
| definition | `reach_from` | 57 | definition reach_from :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_from g roots = (reach_step g ^^ length (all_nodes_list g)) roots" | One round of `reach_step` adds at least one node until closure, so iterating as often as there are nodes saturates. |
| definition | `reach_list` | 60 | definition reach_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_list g = reach_from g (entry_roots g)" |  |
| definition | `dead_list` | 63 | definition dead_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "dead_list g = filter (λv. v ∉ set (reach_list g)) (all_nodes_list g)" |  |
| definition | `nop_edge_list` | 66 | definition nop_edge_list :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "nop_edge_list g = filter (λ(u, a, v). a = EA_Nop) (cfg_intra_list g)" |  |
| definition | `cfg_report` | 74 | definition cfg_report :: "cfg ⇒ nat × nat × nat × nat × nat" where<br>  "cfg_report g =<br>     (length (all_nodes_list g), length (dead_list g), length (cfg_intra_list g),<br>      length (nop_edge_list g), length (cfg_calls_list g))" | `(nodes, dead nodes, intra edges, nop edges, call edges)` --- the row recorded for each regression program, so a compiler change shows up as a diff of these numbers. |
| definition | `prog_cfg` | 81 | definition prog_cfg :: "imp_prog ⇒ cfg" where<br>  "prog_cfg P = compile_prog (prog_table P) (prog_procs P) ''main'' (prog_main P)" |  |
| definition | `p01_skip` | 89 | definition p01_skip :: imp_prog where<br>  "p01_skip = program { void f() { skip } void main() { f() } }" | Each shape is the body of `f`, with `main` reduced to a single call, so the `main` contribution to every row is constant and the differences isolate the shape. |
| definition | `p02_assign` | 92 | definition p02_assign :: imp_prog where<br>  "p02_assign = program { void f() { x := 1 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p03_return` | 95 | definition p03_return :: imp_prog where<br>  "p03_return = program { void f() { return 1 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p04_return_then_dead` | 98 | definition p04_return_then_dead :: imp_prog where<br>  "p04_return_then_dead = program { void f() { return 1; x := 2 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p05_if_both_return` | 101 | definition p05_if_both_return :: imp_prog where<br>  "p05_if_both_return =<br>     program { void f() { if (x &lt; 1) { return 1 } else { return 2 } } void main() { f() } }" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_CallString.thy

> `SeidlEtAl2026`, Example 7: for 1-call-string context-sensitivity, the context set `C` is the call sites of the program plus a distinguished `_main` element, and `context\<^bsub>u,f,args\<^esub> _ _ = u` --- the new context is exactly the call site, ignoring both the calling context and the entered value. `Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Collect` routes `twice`'s two calls by *decoding* the entered formal (`ivl_enterc`, partial tabulation, the paper's Example 8). This theory routes the same program by call site instead: the context type is `cfg_node`, and `route_cs u ctx d ca = u` is exactly the paper's Example 7. Before `enterc` carried the call site (`CFG_Local_Trace.key`, widened for issue `#66`/G1), a call-site-keyed context had no semantic key to be proved sound against; this theory witnesses that the gap is closed.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `route_cs` | 32 | definition route_cs :: "pp ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "route_cs u ctx d ca = u" | `route_cs` ignores the calling context and the entered data entirely, so it commutes with *any* representation map on `'d` --- in particular the executable/abstract refinement `fun_of_st` --- for free. One definition serves both the executable and the abstract routing hook below. |
| datatype | `gk_cs` | 43 | datatype gk_cs = GlobalCS \| SeedCS pp cfg_node | Distinct constructor names from `gk` (`Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship`): both theories are imported here, and `Seed` would otherwise be ambiguous. |
| definition | `twice_cs_eqs` | 55 | definition twice_cs_eqs :: "(pp × cfg_node, gk_cs, (ivl st, ivl st) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "twice_cs_eqs =<br>     side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. GlobalCS) route_cs<br>       (routed_cmb Spoly GlobalCS) (routed_extra twice_cfg Spoly SeedCS GlobalCS)<br>       twice_cfg Spoly bot cinit_ivl_st (restrict_global_st cinit_ivl_st)" | The root context is `cfg_entry` `twice_cfg` --- `main`'s own entry node, the paper's distinguished `_main` element. It is never a call site inside `twice`, so it cannot collide with a routed context. |
| definition | `twice_cs_sol` | 61 | definition twice_cs_sol ::<br>  "(pp × cfg_node) set × (pp × cfg_node + gk_cs ⇒ (ivl st, ivl st) dg_state)" where<br>  "twice_cs_sol = TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve twice_cs_eqs<br>                    (cfg_exit twice_cfg, cfg_entry twice_cfg)" |  |
| definition | `ctx_call1_cs` | 77 | definition ctx_call1_cs :: cfg_node where "ctx_call1_cs = Statement 2" | Unlike `ctx_call1` / `ctx_call2` (`Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship`, decoded from the solved entry store), these contexts are syntactic: no solution lookup, no argument decoding, known before the solver runs. |
| definition | `ctx_call2_cs` | 78 | definition ctx_call2_cs :: cfg_node where "ctx_call2_cs = Statement 3" |  |
| definition | `enterc_cs` | 246 | definition enterc_cs :: "cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ store ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "enterc_cs u ctx s = u" | The semantic context function: exactly `route_cs`, restricted to a concrete `store`. This is the `enterc` the paper's Example 7 asks for, and the widened `key` (`CFG_Local_Trace.thy`, G1) is what lets it be stated at all. |
| abbreviation | `sigma_cs` | 249 | abbreviation sigma_cs :: "pp × cfg_node + gk_cs ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "sigma_cs ≡ fun_of_dg_st ∘ snd twice_cs_sol" |  |
| abbreviation | `gen_cs_abs` | 252 | abbreviation gen_cs_abs :: "(pp × cfg_node, gk_cs, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "gen_cs_abs ≡ side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. GlobalCS) route_cs<br>       (routed_cmb Sabs GlobalCS) (routed_extra twice_cfg Sabs SeedCS GlobalCS) twice_cfg Sabs<br>       (fun_of_st (bot::ivl st)) (fun_of_st cinit_ivl_st) (fun_of_st (restrict_global_st cinit_ivl_st))" |  |
| definition | `ivl_ctx_sg_cs` | 272 | definition ivl_ctx_sg_cs :: "pp × cfg_node + gk_cs ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_ctx_sg_cs k =<br>     (case k of<br>        Inl (v, ctx) ⇒<br>          (if (v, ctx) ∈ fst twice_cs_sol<br>           then locals (sigma_cs (Inl (v, ctx))) ⊔ globs (sigma_cs (Inr GlobalCS))<br>           else bot)<br>      \| Inr _ ⇒ bot)" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_graph_config` | 498 | definition twice_cs_graph_config ::<br>  "(cfg_node, gk_cs, (ivl st, ivl st) dg_state, ivl st) analysis_graph_config" where<br>  "twice_cs_graph_config =<br>    ⦇ local_of = locals,<br>      route = (λu ctx action d. route_cs u ctx d action),<br>      show_context = string_of_cfg_node,<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        let sc = compiled_procedure_scope twice_pi twice_procs ''main'' twice_main<br>          twice_cfg p<br>        in scope_formals sc @ scope_locals sc),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope twice_pi twice_procs ''main'' twice_main<br>          twice_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [],<br>      show_local = (λp ctx vars d. map (λx.<br>... (14 more lines) | The same exporter as `twice_ctx_graph` (`Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship`), instantiated at the call-site context. `string_of_cfg_node` --- the CFG's own node printer --- doubles as `show_context`, since a context *is* a `cfg_node` here: the two routed callee clusters are labelled by the call site that created them, not by a decoded value. |
| definition | `twice_cs_contexts_for_pp` | 528 | definition twice_cs_contexts_for_pp :: "pp ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "twice_cs_contexts_for_pp p =<br>    (if compiled_owner_of twice_pi twice_procs ''main'' twice_main p = ''main''<br>     then [cfg_entry twice_cfg] else [ctx_call1_cs, ctx_call2_cs])" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_local_graph_domain` | 533 | definition twice_cs_local_graph_domain :: "(pp × cfg_node + gk_cs) list" where<br>  "twice_cs_local_graph_domain =<br>    contextual_graph_domain twice_cfg twice_cs_contexts_for_pp" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_seed_keys` | 537 | definition twice_cs_seed_keys :: "gk_cs list" where<br>  "twice_cs_seed_keys =<br>     map (λctx. SeedCS (FunctionEntry ''twice'') ctx) [ctx_call1_cs, ctx_call2_cs]" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_graph_domain` | 541 | definition twice_cs_graph_domain :: "(pp × cfg_node + gk_cs) list" where<br>  "twice_cs_graph_domain =<br>    twice_cs_local_graph_domain @ map Inr twice_cs_seed_keys" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_graph` | 545 | definition twice_cs_graph :: "(cfg_node, gk_cs) analysis_graph" where<br>  "twice_cs_graph =<br>    build_analysis_graph twice_cs_graph_config twice_cfg twice_cs_graph_domain<br>      (snd twice_cs_sol)" |  |
| definition | `twice_cs_dot` | 550 | definition twice_cs_dot :: String.literal where<br>  "twice_cs_dot =<br>    String.implode<br>      (analysis_graph_to_dot twice_cs_graph_config twice_cfg (snd twice_cs_sol)<br>        twice_cs_graph)" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Collect.thy

> The connecting theorem between the routed executable post-solution (`twice_ctx_pp_abs`) and the semantic activation-indexed collecting semantics. It instantiates the generic `activation_collect_sound` and discharges its four obligations --- `ENTRY_G`, `EDGE`, `SEED_G`, `COMB` --- as separate lemmas. Coverage is internalised in the guarded reader `ivl_ctx_sg` (uncovered unknowns denote `{}`), real globals are shared through the single `Global` slot, and the return combine reads the callee exit at the routed context (`ivl_ctx_sg_comb`).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ivl_enterc` | 28 | definition ivl_enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ ivl ⇒ store ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "ivl_enterc u ctx s = ivl_decode (s ''p'')" | A call selects the point abstraction of the callee formal in the entered store. Returns resume the caller context, and the root uses `bot`. `ivl_ctx_sg` joins the routed local slot with the shared global slot expected by `activation_collect_sound`. |
| definition | `ivl_ctx_sg` | 38 | definition ivl_ctx_sg :: "pp × ivl + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_ctx_sg k =<br>     (case k of<br>        Inl (v, ctx) ⇒<br>          (if (v, ctx) ∈ fst twice_ctx_sol<br>           then locals ((fun_of_dg_st ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol) (Inl (v, ctx)))<br>                ⊔ globs ((fun_of_dg_st ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol) (Inr Global))<br>           else bot)<br>      \| Inr _ ⇒ bot)" | The reader is guarded by the *solved domain* `fst twice_ctx_sol`: the solver returns a partial solution, so an unknown outside `vars` is an artefact of the total implementation function and must denote no states. A covered `Inl` slot reads the transported local slot joined with its context's real-global slot; every other key (uncovered local, or any `Inr` global key, which `activation_collect_sound` never consults) denotes `⊥`. Uncovered points thus have empty concretization by construction, with no appeal to solver leastness. |
| abbreviation | `sigma_abs` | 57 | abbreviation sigma_abs :: "pp × ivl + gk ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "sigma_abs ≡ fun_of_dg_st ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol" | One name for the transported abstract solution and the abstract routed generator that `twice_ctx_pp_abs` is a post-solution of. The two projections below --- the per-slot value bound (`eq ≤ σ(Inl …)`) and the side-effect bound (`sides_of_rhs ≤ σ`) --- are the single elimination of `part_post_solution` that `EDGE`, `SEED_G`, and `COMB` all read through; the post-solution is never unfolded again inside a semantic obligation. |
| abbreviation | `gen_abs` | 60 | abbreviation gen_abs :: "(pp × ivl, gk, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "gen_abs ≡ side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. Global) route_abs_gen<br>       (routed_cmb Sabs Global) (routed_extra twice_cfg Sabs Seed Global) twice_cfg Sabs<br>       (fun_of_st (bot::ivl st)) (fun_of_st cinit_ivl_st) (fun_of_st (restrict_global_st cinit_ivl_st))" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship.thy

> The generalized D/G generator is instantiated with routed context hooks. Each call to `twice` receives the abstract entry value of formal `p` as its context: • `twice(3)` uses context `[3,3]` and computes `#ret = [6,6]` and `x = [6,6]`; • `twice(10)` uses context `[10,10]` and computes `#ret = [20,20]` and `y = [20,20]`. The two repeated calls remain separate, whereas unit context joins their formal and return values. This entry-value key is finite for the two constant call sites. A general interval analysis needs a finite canonical context representation because arbitrary interval states may grow under recursion and widening.

Distinct constructors prevent the two roles from sharing a solver unknown. |

(ce, ca, k)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" | Outgoing call edges of a node, as callee entry / call action / continuation. The procedure-aware `calls` relation records all three on the edge, so no separate matching relation is consulted. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Multi_Call_Regression.thy

> `twice_cfg`'s two calls share one callee entry and never share a call site, so it cannot exercise the case `routed_cmb`'s routing comment warns about: a call site with more than one outgoing call edge, where reconstructing the triggering call action from the call site alone (`call_successor_list`, taking its head) is ambiguous, while `return_call_action_list` --- keyed on the return's continuation, not the call site --- picks the exact edge unambiguously. `multi_call_cfg` is a minimal hand-built witness: one call site, `Statement 0`, with two outgoing edges to two different callees and two different continuations.

| definition | `mc_f2` | 20 | definition mc_f2 :: pname where "mc_f2 = ''f2''" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_Flagship.thy

> **The complete story in one self-contained theory.** An VIMP program (given inline below) is compiled to a CFG; the generic D/G framework generates an equation system; the *verified* top-down solver *computes* an interval solution inside Isabelle (with interval widening for termination); the computed solution is certified a partial post-solution by the solver's own correctness theorem; it is transported value-wise to the abstract D/G semantics; and the generic collecting-soundness theorem concludes that the computed abstraction over-approximates the concrete collecting semantics at every program point. \<^verbatim>\<open> VIMP source \| compile_prog (section 2) v CFG \| dg_gen_of (section 4) v D/G equation system \| verified solver, by eval (section 5) v computed interval solution sigma \| solver correctness theorem (section 6) v part_post_solution sigma \| part_post_solution_dg_st_to_abs (section 7) v abstract post-solution \| ivl_dg_post_solution_collect_sound (section 8) v ltr_collect subset gamma(sigma) --- x in [0,20] (section 9) \| compiler-correctness simulation (section 10) v every VIMP source run is bounded by sigma \<close> **Every step is machine-checked, and the result is informative:** the analysis discovers the loop invariant `x in [0,20]`, the body bound `x in [0,19]`, and the exact exit value `x in [20,20]` --- not `top`. Section 10 lifts soundness from the collecting semantics to *actual VIMP source runs* via the compiler-correctness simulation; section 11 exhibits an explicit reachable state so the guarantee is visibly *not vacuous*; section 12 emits an analysis-annotated GraphViz rendering. It reuses, without duplicating: the executable interval transfer `ivl_tf_st` (`Ivl_Exec`), the executable D/G bridge (`Exec_DG_Bridge`), the native interval D/G soundness endpoint (`Interval_DG`), the compiler-correctness simulation (`Source_Activation_Sound`), and the vendored warrowing solver.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `flagship_prog` | 73 | definition flagship_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "flagship_prog = program { void main() { x := 0; while (x &lt; 20) { x := x + 1 } } }" | A bounded counting loop: initialise `x` to `0`, increment while `x < 20`. On exit `x = 20`. No procedures, no globals; `x` is a single flow-sensitive local. The analysis must *discover* the bound, not assume it. |
| definition | `flagship_pi` | 90 | definition flagship_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "flagship_pi = prog_table flagship_prog" | The declaration environment holds exactly `main`: there are no other procedures, and `wf_compile_input` requires the entry procedure to be declared. |

Locals seed at `cinit_ivl_st` (globals `[0,0]`, locals `top`); the flow-insensitive global slot seeds at `restrict_global_st cinit_ivl_st` (bottom on locals, so it never pollutes the local `x` through the diagonal read). |

()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''unit''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_locals (compiled_procedure_scope Map.empty [] prog_main_name (prog_main flagship_prog)<br>          flagship_cfg p)),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope Map.empty [] prog_main_name (prog_main flagship_prog)<br>          flagship_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ vars d. map (λx.<br>        x @ ''='' @ string_of_ivl (lookup_st d x)) vars),<br>... The `ML_val` below prints the DOT as a plain string; paste it into any GraphViz renderer (`dot -Tpng`) to view the analysed loop, each node labelled with the loop-invariant interval the verified solver computed. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_IP_Flagship.thy

> This is the non-context IP baseline: every call to `twice` is analyzed under a single, shared abstract state at `FunctionEntry ''twice''`, regardless of which call site reached it. It is the flagship the context-sensitive siblings sharpen -- `Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship` routes by the entered argument's abstract value, `Example_Interval_DG_CallString` routes by call site (a 1-CFA-style call string) -- so run this file first to see the precision loss two calls to the same procedure with different arguments incur when their entry states are forced to join, then compare against the routed variants.
