### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_Loop_Coverage.thy

> A full program carried end to end through the interval analyzer: `x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 }` The interval domain proves the bounded invariant `0 <= x <= 20` (i.e. `x ∈ [0, 20]`) at the loop head over every reaching store. Both bounds are interval-specific: the lower bound `0` is the joined initial value, and the upper bound `20` comes from guard refinement -- `assume_ivl` narrows `x` to `[.., 19]` on entering the body (`x < 20`), so after `x := x + 1` and the join with the initial `[0,0]` the loop head stabilises at `[0,20]` with no widening needed. The Sign lattice expresses neither bound (it collapses `[0,0]` joined with `[1,1]` to sign top). Certified backward-analysis story: *Example_Guard_Refinement* isolates the guard step; this theory carries the same `assume_ivl` transfers through the full CFG to the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem. Executable mirror (Kleene / warrowing TD on *ivl st*, eval only): *Exec_Ivl_Run*.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `loop_prog` | 34 | definition loop_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "loop_prog = program {<br>     void main() { x := 0; while (x &lt; 20) { x := x + 1 } }<br>   }" | A full program carried end to end through the interval analyzer: `x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 }` The interval domain proves the bounded invariant `0 <= x <= 20` (i.e. `x ∈ [0, 20]`) at the loop head over every reaching store. Both bounds are interval-specific: the lower bound `0` is the joined initial value, and the upper bound `20` comes from guard refinement -- `assume_ivl` narrows `x` to `[.., 19]` on entering the body (`x < 20`), so after `x := x + 1` and the join with the initial `[0,0]` the loop head stabilises at `[0,20]` with no widening needed. The Sign lattice expresses neither bound (it collapses `[0,0]` joined with `[1,1]` to sign top). Certified backward-analysis story: *Example_Guard_Refinement* isolates the guard step; this theory carries the same `assume_ivl` transfers through the full CFG to the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem. Executable mirror (Kleene / warrowing TD on *ivl st*, eval only): *Exec_Ivl_Run*. |
| abbreviation | `loop_cfg` | 41 | abbreviation "loop_cfg ≡<br>  compile_prog (prog_table loop_prog) (prog_procs loop_prog) prog_main_name (prog_main loop_prog)" |  |
| definition | `loop_s0` | 75 | definition loop_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "loop_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" |  |
| definition | `loop_env` | 88 | definition loop_env :: "pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "loop_env v =<br>     (if v = Statement 2 then (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)(''x'' := Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 19))<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 1, Statement 3}<br>        then (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)(''x'' := Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 20))<br>      else (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf))" | The loop head (node 1) stabilises at `[0,20]`: the join of `x := 0` flowing in directly from node 0 and `x := x + 1` flowing back from the body. Guard refinement narrows the body entry (node 2) to `[0,19]`; applying the body's own assignment to that value gives `[1,20]`, which joined with the initial `[0,0]` is exactly the loop-head value -- a finite (non-widened) fixpoint. Neither intermediate join operand needs its own node: the continuation-passing compiler routes both edges directly into the loop head, so `rhs` computes the join on the fly. Other variables stay at the full interval. |
| abbreviation | `loop_body_entry` | 145 | abbreviation "loop_body_entry ≡ Statement 2" | Edge from node 2 to node 3 is `EA_Assume` on *x < 20*. The body-entry interval *[0,19]* in `loop_env` is exactly `assume_ivl` applied at the loop head --- not widening and not join alone (identity assume would keep *[0,20]*; see *Example_Guard_Refinement*). |
| abbreviation | `loop_head` | 160 | abbreviation "loop_head ≡ Statement 1" | The loop head is the assume node where ``x < 20`` is checked. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_Side_Proc_Global.thy

> Interval instance of the side-effecting interprocedural witness: the same `inc_pi` program (a single call to procedure p incrementing the global ``''Gx''``) carried through the `side_analyse_eff` solver at the interval domain. Demonstrates the soundness scaffold is domain-generic by reusing it on a second, infinite-height numeric domain.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `side_proc_global_ivl_s0` | 21 | definition side_proc_global_ivl_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "side_proc_global_ivl_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | Initial state: every variable -- including the globals -- starts at the full interval ``Ivl MinInf PlusInf`` (top), the interval analogue of sign top. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Proc_Call.thy

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `main_cfg_name` | 13 | definition main_cfg_name :: pname where<br>  "main_cfg_name = ''main''" |  |
| definition | `inc_body` | 28 | definition inc_body :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "inc_body = imp ⟦ Gx := Gx + 1 ⟧" | Two parameterless procedures communicate through the global variable ``''Gx''``. Variable names starting with `G` are global: they survive call-frame restore while locals are reset to zero. • `inc`: adds 1 to `Gx`. • `sqr`: replaces `Gx` with its square. Main program: `Gx := 4; call inc; call sqr` terminates with `Gx = 25` since `(4 + 1)^2 = 25`. |
| definition | `sqr_body` | 31 | definition sqr_body :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "sqr_body = imp ⟦ Gx := Gx * Gx ⟧" |  |
| definition | `proc_pi` | 34 | definition proc_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "proc_pi = (λ_. None)(''inc'' := Some (proc_decl_of [] inc_body), ''sqr'' := Some (proc_decl_of [] sqr_body))" |  |
| definition | `main_prog` | 37 | definition main_prog :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "main_prog = imp ⟦<br>     Gx := 4;<br>     inc();<br>     sqr()<br>   ⟧" |  |
| definition | `main_procs` | 44 | definition main_procs :: "pname list" where<br>  "main_procs = [''inc'', ''sqr'']" |  |
| abbreviation | `main_cfg` | 137 | abbreviation "main_cfg ≡ compile_prog proc_pi [''inc'', ''sqr''] main_cfg_name main_prog" | Compile `main_prog` together with its two procedure bodies. `compile_prog` first lays out `inc_body` at statements 0--1 and `sqr_body` at statements 2--3, then compiles the main body starting at statement 4. Each procedure is bracketed by its own `FunctionEntry` / `FunctionResult`; each call edge names its callee entry and its continuation, so the two calls are *5 -> inc, continue at 6* and *6 -> sqr, continue at 7*. |
| definition | `main_prog_s0` | 187 | definition main_prog_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "main_prog_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | The initial abstract state maps every variable to the full interval. |
| definition | `main_prog_env` | 207 | definition main_prog_env :: "pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "main_prog_env v x =<br>     (if v ∈ {Statement 5, FunctionEntry ''inc'', Statement 0} ∧ x = ''Gx''<br>        then Ivl (Fin 4) (Fin 4)<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 1, FunctionResult ''inc'', Statement 6,<br>                   FunctionEntry ''sqr'', Statement 2} ∧ x = ''Gx''<br>        then Ivl (Fin 5) (Fin 5)<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 3, FunctionResult ''sqr'', Statement 7,<br>                   FunctionResult ''main''} ∧ x = ''Gx''<br>        then Ivl (Fin 25) (Fin 25)<br>      else Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | Abstract environment: each program point is assigned an interval state. The assignment propagates *Gx = [4,4]* after *Gx := 4*, *Gx = [5,5]* after *inc* executes, and *Gx = [25,25]* after *sqr* executes. All local variables remain unconstrained throughout. Node groupings: *4* -- main body entry, before *Gx := 4*; *5* -- after *Gx := 4*, which is also the call site to inc; *0* -- inc body entry (reached through the call edge from 5); *1* -- inc body exit, feeding ``FunctionResult ''inc''``; *6* -- continuation after inc, which is also the call site to sqr; *2*, *3* -- sqr body entry and exit; *7* -- continuation after sqr, feeding ``FunctionResult ''main''``. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Exec_Ivl_Run.thy

> Same program as *Example_Interval_Loop_Coverage* in session `Voblint_Formalization`: *x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1*"}. The executable transfer `ivl_tf_st` applies the same backward guard filters as `assume_ivl` (via `assume_ivl_st` / `bfilter_ivl_st`) on `EA_Assume` edges. Node~2 therefore reads *[0,19]* because *x < 20* refines *x* at the loop head --- not because of widening. This theory evaluates two fixpoint engines on `side_cfg_T_eff_st`: bounded Kleene iteration on `eq`, and `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve` (pointwise interval widening on `"ivl st"` for solver termination). The example uses the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `loop_prog` | 26 | definition loop_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "loop_prog = program {<br>     void main() { x := 0; while (x &lt; 20) { x := x + 1 } }<br>   }" | Same program as *Example_Interval_Loop_Coverage* in session `Voblint_Formalization`: *x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1*"}. The executable transfer `ivl_tf_st` applies the same backward guard filters as `assume_ivl` (via `assume_ivl_st` / `bfilter_ivl_st`) on `EA_Assume` edges. Node~2 therefore reads *[0,19]* because *x < 20* refines *x* at the loop head --- not because of widening. This theory evaluates two fixpoint engines on `side_cfg_T_eff_st`: bounded Kleene iteration on `eq`, and `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve` (pointwise interval widening on `"ivl st"` for solver termination). The example uses the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem. |
| definition | `loop_cfg` | 31 | definition loop_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "loop_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         {(FunctionEntry ''main'', EA_Nop, Statement 0),<br>          (Statement 0, EA_Assign ''x'' (N 0), Statement 1),<br>          (Statement 1, EA_Assume (Less (V ''x'') (N 20)), Statement 2),<br>          (Statement 1, EA_AssumeNot (Less (V ''x'') (N 20)), Statement 3),<br>          (Statement 2, EA_Assign ''x'' (Plus (V ''x'') (N 1)), Statement 1),<br>          (Statement 3, EA_Ret None ''main'', FunctionResult ''main'')},<br>       calls = {},<br>       cfg_entry = FunctionEntry ''main'' ⦈" |  |
| definition | `loop_ivl_eqs` | 53 | definition loop_ivl_eqs :: "(pp, unit, ivl st) eqsT" where<br>  "loop_ivl_eqs = side_cfg_T_eff_st loop_cfg ivl_etf_st bot cinit_ivl_st ()" |  |
| definition | `loop_sig0` | 56 | definition loop_sig0 :: "pp + unit ⇒ ivl st" where<br>  "loop_sig0 k =<br>     (case k of Inl _ ⇒ bot \| Inr () ⇒ restrict_global_st cinit_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `loop_kleene_step` | 60 | definition loop_kleene_step :: "(pp + unit ⇒ ivl st) ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ ivl st)" where<br>  "loop_kleene_step sig =<br>     (λk. case k of<br>        Inl v ⇒ eq loop_ivl_eqs v sig<br>      \| Inr () ⇒ sig (Inr ()))" |  |
| fun | `loop_iter_sig` | 66 | fun loop_iter_sig :: "nat ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ ivl st) ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ ivl st)" where<br>  "loop_iter_sig 0 sig = sig"<br>\| "loop_iter_sig (Suc n) sig = loop_iter_sig n (loop_kleene_step sig)" |  |
| definition | `loop_ivl_sol` | 70 | definition loop_ivl_sol :: "pp set × (pp + unit ⇒ ivl st)" where<br>  "loop_ivl_sol =<br>     ({FunctionEntry ''main'', FunctionResult ''main''}<br>        ∪ Statement ` {0, 1, 2, 3},<br>      loop_iter_sig 100 loop_sig0)" |  |
| definition | `loop_ivl_at` | 76 | definition loop_ivl_at :: "pp ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "loop_ivl_at pp = lookup_st (snd loop_ivl_sol (Inl pp)) ''x''" |  |
| definition | `loop_ivl_td_sol` | 93 | definition loop_ivl_td_sol :: "pp set × (pp + unit ⇒ ivl st)" where<br>  "loop_ivl_td_sol = TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve loop_ivl_eqs (cfg_exit loop_cfg)" |  |
| definition | `loop_ivl_td_at` | 96 | definition loop_ivl_td_at :: "pp ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "loop_ivl_td_at pp = lookup_st (snd loop_ivl_td_sol (Inl pp)) ''x''" |  |

### src/Examples/Mixed/Example_Relational_DG_Demo.thy

> `../../Analysis/Instances/Mixed/Rel_Order_Domain.thy` interprets `sound_dg_spec` over `relc`, a non-`abs_state` relational carrier, with zero DG-framework changes -- the mathematical half of the claim. This file is the executable half: the same CFG, the same generic `dg_gen_of` generator, and the same vendored solver that runs Interval also run `relc`, end to end, with a genuinely different observable result. **Program.** `if (x < y) { z := 1 } else { z := 0 }`, with `x`/`y` left entirely unconstrained at entry (no prior assignment). This is deliberately the case where Interval learns nothing from the guard: `x < y` narrows `x`'s interval only using `y`'s *current* upper bound, and vice versa; with both at `[-inf,+inf]`, the guard supplies no new bound for either variable. `relc` instead records the pair `(x,y)` directly, because `assume_step` reads the guard's syntactic shape, not either variable's current abstract value.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `demo_program` | 37 | definition demo_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "demo_program = program { <br>   void main() { <br>     if (x &lt; y) <br>        { z := 1 } <br>     else <br>        { z := 0 }<br>     } <br>}" |  |
| definition | `demo_pi` | 49 | definition demo_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "demo_pi = prog_table demo_program" |  |
| definition | `demo_cfg` | 52 | definition demo_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "demo_cfg = compile_prog demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program) prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program)" |  |
| definition | `demo_ivl_eqs` | 79 | definition demo_ivl_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (ivl st, ivl st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "demo_ivl_eqs =<br>     dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st ivl_tf_st ivl_enter_st) demo_cfg<br>       bot top_ivl_st (restrict_global_st top_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `demo_ivl_sol` | 85 | definition demo_ivl_sol ::<br>  "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (ivl st, ivl st) dg_state)" where<br>  "demo_ivl_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve demo_ivl_eqs (cfg_exit demo_cfg, ())" |  |
| definition | `demo_rel_eqs` | 100 | definition demo_rel_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (relc, relc) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "demo_rel_eqs = dg_gen_of rel_order_spec demo_cfg bot top_relc top_relc" | `rel_order_spec` is already both the sound *and* the executable specification -- `relc` needed no `Exec_St.thy`-style refinement layer, so `dg_gen_of` is applied to it directly, with no bridging step and no parallel generator. |
| definition | `demo_rel_sol` | 104 | definition demo_rel_sol ::<br>  "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (relc, relc) dg_state)" where<br>  "demo_rel_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve demo_rel_eqs (cfg_exit demo_cfg, ())" |  |

()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''unit''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_locals (compiled_procedure_scope demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program)<br>          prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program) demo_cfg p)),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program)<br>          prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program) demo_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ _ d. (9 more lines) | Same CFG, same rendering backend, this time with each node labelled by the `relc` value the solver computed there -- `string_of_relc` is the `relc` analogue of `string_of_ivl`, and `local_of = locals` is the same projection the flagship Interval example uses. |
| definition | `demo_graph_domain` | 191 | definition demo_graph_domain :: "(pp × unit + unit) list" where<br>  "demo_graph_domain = contextual_graph_domain demo_cfg (λ_. [()])" |  |

### src/Examples/Parity/Example_Parity_DG_Flagship.thy

> **Second domain, same registration.** This theory is the E1 validation of the domain-registration API: parity registers through the `unit_dg_exec_analysis` locale (as `parity_reg` in `DG_Domain_Registration`) with *no* copied `Hstep`, `Hcomb`, `strategy_tree`, `Inl`/`Inr`, or manual post-solution transport lemmas. An IMP2 program is compiled to a CFG; the generic D/G framework generates the equation system; the *verified* always-join solver *computes* a parity solution inside Isabelle (finite lattice, no widening needed); and the single registered endpoint `parity_reg.run_source_sound` lifts the result to actual source runs. The result is informative: the analysis *discovers* that `x` is even at every program point (`x = 0` initially, then `x := x + 2` preserves parity), so the loop invariant `x` even holds without any guard refinement --- parity ignores the guard.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `parity_program` | 42 | definition parity_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "parity_program = program {<br><br>      int G;<br><br>      void main() { <br>        x := 0;<br>        y:=1;<br>        while (x &lt; 20) { <br>          x := x + 2; <br>          y:=y + 1 <br>        }; <br>        G:= x + y<br>      }<br>}" | A bounded counting loop that increments by two: initialise `x` to `0`, add `2` while `x < 20`. No procedures beyond `main`, no globals; `x` is a single flow-sensitive local. The parity of `x` stays even at every reachable point regardless of the guard, which the analysis must discover, not assume. |
| definition | `parity_prog` | 58 | definition parity_prog :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "parity_prog = prog_main parity_program" |  |
| definition | `parity_pi` | 61 | definition parity_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "parity_pi = prog_table parity_program" |  |
| definition | `parity_cfg` | 71 | definition parity_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "parity_cfg = compile_prog parity_pi [] ''main'' parity_prog" | The source compiles to an interprocedural CFG by `compile_prog`, exactly like the interval flagship's counting loop --- same topology, only the increment differs. |
| definition | `parity_eqs` | 121 | definition parity_eqs :: "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (parity st, parity st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "parity_eqs = dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st parity_tf_st parity_enter_st) parity_cfg<br>     bot cinit_parity_st (restrict_global_st cinit_parity_st)" |  |
| definition | `parity_sol` | 131 | definition parity_sol :: "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (parity st, parity st) dg_state)" where<br>  "parity_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve parity_eqs (cfg_exit parity_cfg, ())" |  |
| definition | `parity_graph_config` | 249 | definition parity_graph_config ::<br>  "(unit, unit, (parity st, parity st) dg_state, parity st) analysis_graph_config" where<br>  "parity_graph_config =<br>    ⦇ local_of = locals,<br>      route = (λ_ _ _ _. ()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''unit''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_locals (compiled_procedure_scope parity_pi [] ''main'' parity_prog<br>          parity_cfg p)),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope parity_pi [] ''main'' parity_prog<br>          parity_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [''G''],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ vars d.<br>        map (λx.<br>... (12 more lines) | A DOT rendering of `parity_cfg` with each node annotated by the computed parity for `x`. The `ML_val` below prints the DOT as a plain string; paste it into any GraphViz renderer (`dot -Tpng`) to view the analysed loop, each node labelled with the loop-invariant parity the verified solver computed. |

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Mixed_Flow_Sign.thy

> Demonstrates the top-level collecting-semantics theorem applied to the sign domain on the `inc_pi` program (procedure p increments the global Gx). The effectful transfer record is the domain interface here. Its soundness, cone compatibility, and threefold monotonicity are discharged by the Sign domain theory. Two obligations remain program- or run-specific: dom: solver termination -- no static proof from the equation system S_sound: S <= gamma(s0) -- caller's initial stores must fit the abstract entry

STop)" | Demonstrates the top-level collecting-semantics theorem applied to the sign domain on the `inc_pi` program (procedure p increments the global Gx). ### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Side_Branch_Calls.thy

> A richer end-to-end witness than the single-statement `Example_Side_Execute`: a procedure `compute` with an `if`/`else` on a global input, called twice from `main` with different inputs, followed by an arithmetic use of the result. In C: \begin{verbatim} int Ginput; int Gresult; // globals (G-prefixed) void compute() { int x = 1; // local if (Ginput > 0) x = x + 1; else x = x + 2; Gresult = x; } int main() { int r = 0; // local call counter Ginput = 5; compute(); r = r + 1; Ginput = -3; compute(); r = r + 1; Gout = 100 * Gresult; // language has no '/', so use '*' } \end{verbatim} Two modelling notes. The expression language @{type aexp} has `Plus` / `Minus` / `Times` but no division, so `100 / Gresult` is written as the multiplication `100 * Gresult`. Variables whose name starts with `G` are global (`is_global`); *x* is local to *compute*, *r* to *main*.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `branch_prog_mnm` | 35 | definition branch_prog_mnm :: pname where "branch_prog_mnm = ''main''" | A richer end-to-end witness than the single-statement `Example_Side_Execute`: a procedure `compute` with an `if`/`else` on a global input, called twice from `main` with different inputs, followed by an arithmetic use of the result. In C: \begin{verbatim} int Ginput; int Gresult; // globals (G-prefixed) void compute() { int x = 1; // local if (Ginput > 0) x = x + 1; else x = x + 2; Gresult = x; } int main() { int r = 0; // local call counter Ginput = 5; compute(); r = r + 1; Ginput = -3; compute(); r = r + 1; Gout = 100 * Gresult; // language has no '/', so use '*' } \end{verbatim} Two modelling notes. The expression language @{type aexp} has `Plus` / `Minus` / `Times` but no division, so `100 / Gresult` is written as the multiplication `100 * Gresult`. Variables whose name starts with `G` are global (`is_global`); *x* is local to *compute*, *r* to *main*. |
| definition | `branch_prog` | 37 | definition branch_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "branch_prog = program {<br>     int Ginput, Gresult, Gout;<br><br>     void compute() {<br>       x := 1;<br>       if (0 &lt; Ginput) { x := x + 1 } else { x := x + 2 };<br>       Gresult := x<br>     }<br>     void main() {<br>       r := 0;<br>       Ginput := 5;<br>       compute();<br>       r := r + 1;<br>       Ginput := 0 - 3;<br>... (5 more lines) |  |

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Side_Proc_Global.thy

> Side-effecting interprocedural witness: `inc_pi` with a single call to procedure p. Its soundness uses the effectful side TD solver.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `side_proc_global_s0` | 20 | definition side_proc_global_s0 :: "sign abs_state" where<br>  "side_proc_global_s0 = (λ_. STop)" |  |
| definition | `inc_procs` | 38 | definition inc_procs :: "pname list" where<br>  "inc_procs = [''p'']" |  |

### src/Examples/Sign/Exec_Sign_DG_Run.thy

> An end-to-end certified run on the carrier-opaque D/G equation system, registered through the `unit_dg_exec_analysis` locale (interpreted as `sign_reg` in `DG_Domain_Registration` from just `sign_is_sound_transfer` and `sign_tf_st_commute`). A concrete call-free Sign program is compiled to a CFG; the executable D/G generator (`dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st sign_tf_st)`, values in `(sign st, sign st) dg_state`) is handed to the vendored always-join TD-side solver; the solver *computes* a partial post-solution. The final theorem `dgEx_source_run_sound` turns the single `by eval` solver success directly into a source-level guarantee, matching the pattern in `Example_Interval_DG_Flagship`: no transport lemma, `part_post_solution`, `solve_dom`, or `fun_of_dg_st` appears in this file's own proofs. It depends on the *computed* solver result `snd dgEx_sol`, not on any hand-written candidate solution.

### src/Examples/Tooling/Example_Mixed_Sign_Interval_GraphViz.thy

> The program compiles through the ordinary VIMP-to-CFG pipeline. The variable *''x''* is local according to `is_global`. Per-point answer unknowns carry Sign stores, while the single side unknown carries an Interval store chosen by this analysis. The side slot is called `globs` by the generic routing layer, but it is not an VIMP global-variable store. Starting from *x = 0*, execution visits *x = -1* and finishes at *x = 2*. The exit Sign answer is therefore positive. The shared Interval invariant joins all three values and is *[-1, 2]*. The DOT rendering displays the flow-sensitive Sign answers at CFG nodes and the flow-insensitive Interval invariant in a separate cluster.

The variable *''x''* is local according to `is_global`. Per-point answer unknowns carry Sign stores, while the single side unknown carries an Interval store chosen by this analysis. The side slot is called `globs` by the generic routing layer, but it is not an VIMP global-variable store. Starting from *x = 0*, execution visits *x = -1* and finishes at *x = 2*. The exit Sign answer is therefore positive. The shared Interval invariant joins all three values and is *[-1, 2]*. ### src/Examples/Tooling/Example_Strategy_Tree_Demo.thy

> A minimal, domain-independent demonstration that `strategy_tree` is a small effect language on its own -- no abstract domain, CFG, or context involved. `answer` returns a value and `seqcomp_tree` sequences two trees: exactly `return`/`>>=` for this monad (`Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy`), written below with `do` notation (`Strategy_Tree_Do.thy` registers `seqcomp_tree` as this monad's bind, so `do { x ← t; k x }` parses to exactly `seqcomp_tree t k`). `fib_tree n` builds the whole finite computation tree for `fib n` before any evaluation. `strategy_tree` itself gains no recursion primitive -- the recursion lives in the Isabelle function that builds the tree, exactly as every equation-authoring definition in this codebase builds a finite tree per unknown ahead of the solver run.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| fun | `fib_tree` | 31 | fun fib_tree :: "nat ⇒ ('x, 'g, nat) strategy_tree" where<br>  "fib_tree 0 = answer 0"<br>\| "fib_tree (Suc 0) = answer 1"<br>\| "fib_tree (Suc (Suc n)) =<br>     do {<br>       a ← fib_tree (Suc n);<br>       b ← fib_tree n;<br>       answer (a + b)<br>     }" |  |

## Formalization

### src/Formalization/Pipeline/Run_Analysis_Sound.thy

> One reusable theorem replaces the per-example chain \<open>solve_c success -> solve_dom -> partial_post_solution -> executable-to-abstract transport -> collecting soundness -> source-level soundness\<close>. \<open>This theorem bundles partial correctness and source-level soundness. It does not establish that `solve_c` returns.\<close> The caller supplies `solve_c ... ≠ None` (typically a code-generated `by eval` fact), and the bundle turns that single success into a source-level guarantee. Termination of the vendored solver is not a theorem of this development. The result is *query-parametric*: the reached program point `v` is existential and matched to where the run actually is, and the D/G collecting endpoint `dg_post_solution_collect_sound_ltr` holds at every covered point, so no premature specialisation to `cfg_exit` is needed. **Why the solver step stays a one-line adapter.** The step \<open>solve_c success -> part_post_solution\<close> lives in the vendored locale `TD_side_upd_rule` as `part_post_solution_of_solve_c`, whose unknown type is rigid inside that locale and cannot be tied to the D/G unknown type `pp × unit`. So the executable-to-abstract transport lives in the *global* theorem below, and the caller feeds it the executable `part_post_solution` obtained from a single `by eval` success via that adapter.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| locale | `unit_dg_exec_analysis` | 187 | locale unit_dg_exec_analysis =<br>  fixes tf :: "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer"<br>    and tf_st :: "edge_action ⇒ 'a st ⇒ 'a st"<br>    and enter_st :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'a st ⇒ 'a st"<br>    and solve :: "(pp × unit, unit, ('a st, 'a st) dg_state) eqsT<br>                   ⇒ pp × unit<br>                   ⇒ (pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ ('a st, 'a st) dg_state)"<br>    and solve_c :: "(pp × unit, unit, ('a st, 'a st) dg_state) eqsT<br>                   ⇒ pp × unit<br>                   ⇒ ((pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ ('a st, 'a st) dg_state)) option"<br>  assumes tf_sound: "sound_transfer tf"<br>    and tf_commute: "⋀a s. fun_of_st (tf_st a s) = apply_tf tf a (fun_of_st s)"<br>    and enter_commute: "⋀xs es s. fun_of_st (enter_st xs es s) = tf_enter tf xs es (fun_of_st s)"<br>    and ret_none: "⋀p. tf_st (EA_Ret None p) = tf_st EA_Nop"<br>    and ret_some: "⋀a p. tf_st (EA_Ret (Some a) p) = tf_st (EA_Assign ret_var a)"<br>... (3 more lines) | A registered diagonal analysis supplies only *essential mathematics* --- a sound abstract transfer `tf` and its executable mirror `tf_st` with the primitive commutation --- plus the vendored solver's `part_post_solution_of_solve_c` adapter for its chosen update rule (`solve` / `solve_c`). It obtains a reusable executable source-soundness theorem `run_source_sound` and a semantic concretization accessor `gamma`, with no `dg_spec_step` trees, `fun_of_dg_st`, `part_post_solution`, or transport lemmas exposed to callers. |
| definition | `gamma` | 215 | definition gamma :: "(pp × unit + unit ⇒ ('a st, 'a st) dg_state) ⇒ pp ⇒ store set"<br>  where "gamma sigma_st v = sound_dg_spec.dg_gamma gamma_unit (fun_of_dg_st ∘ sigma_st) v" | `gamma` is the caller-facing concretization at `v`: convert the executable post-solution `sigma_st` to its semantic function via `fun_of_dg_st`, then read off the set of stores the DG framework's own `dg_gamma` assigns it, instantiated at this locale's context-insensitive `gamma_unit`. This is the accessor `run_source_sound` states its soundness guarantee in terms of, so no `dg_spec_step`/`fun_of_dg_st`/`part_post_solution` plumbing reaches the caller. |

## VIMP

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Expr.thy

> This theory also re-exports the reflexive-transitive closure used by the procedure small-step relation.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `aval` | 12 | fun aval :: "aexp =&gt; store =&gt; int" where<br>    "aval (N n)     s  = n"<br>  \| "aval (V x)     s  = s x"<br>  \| "aval (Plus  a b) s  = aval a s + aval b s"<br>  \| "aval (Minus a b) s  = aval a s - aval b s"<br>  \| "aval (Times a b) s  = aval a s * aval b s" |  |
| fun | `bval` | 19 | fun bval :: "bexp =&gt; store =&gt; bool" where<br>    "bval (Bc v)      s  = v"<br>  \| "bval (Not b)     s  = (¬ bval b s)"<br>  \| "bval (And b1 b2) s  = (bval b1 s ∧ bval b2 s)"<br>  \| "bval (Or  b1 b2) s  = (bval b1 s ∨ bval b2 s)"<br>  \| "bval (Less a b)  s  = (aval a s &lt; aval b s)"<br>  \| "bval (Eq   a b)  s  = (aval a s = aval b s)" |  |
| fun | `aexp_mentions_where` | 38 | fun aexp_mentions_where :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ aexp ⇒ bool" where<br>  "aexp_mentions_where P (N _) = False"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (V x) = P x"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Plus a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Minus a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Times a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)" | Both "does this expression mention a global?" and "does this expression mention variable x?" are the same syntax-directed walk, differing only in the leaf predicate on the mentioned name. Defining the walk once as *_where P* and instantiating *P* to `is_global` (below) or to an equality test (`VIMP_Proc.aexp_mentions`) avoids two copies of the same recursion. |
| fun | `bexp_mentions_where` | 45 | fun bexp_mentions_where :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ bexp ⇒ bool" where<br>  "bexp_mentions_where P (Bc _) = False"<br>\| "bexp_mentions_where P (Not b) = bexp_mentions_where P b"<br>\| "bexp_mentions_where P (And b1 b2) = (bexp_mentions_where P b1 ∨ bexp_mentions_where P b2)"<br>\| "bexp_mentions_where P (Or b1 b2) = (bexp_mentions_where P b1 ∨ bexp_mentions_where P b2)"<br>\| "bexp_mentions_where P (Less a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)"<br>\| "bexp_mentions_where P (Eq a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)" |  |
| definition | `aexp_mentions_global` | 55 | definition aexp_mentions_global :: "aexp ⇒ bool" where<br>  "aexp_mentions_global = aexp_mentions_where is_global" |  |
| definition | `bexp_mentions_global` | 58 | definition bexp_mentions_global :: "bexp ⇒ bool" where<br>  "bexp_mentions_global = bexp_mentions_where is_global"<br><br>lemmas mentions_global_defs [simp] =<br>  aexp_mentions_global_def bexp_mentions_global_def |  |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Globals.thy

> The store is a single `vname => int` function; the locals/globals split is a naming convention on top of it, not a separate representation. `is_global` names the split; `combine_states` `<s\|t>` takes locals from `s` and globals from `t`. This is the splitting a procedure call needs on both ends: on entry the caller's store is saved and locals reset (`enter_state`), on exit the callee's globals are kept and the caller's locals restored (`<caller\|callee>`). Self-contained: depends only on the store, not on `com`, small-step, or the CFG.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `pname` | 21 | type_synonym pname = string | The store is a single `vname => int` function; the locals/globals split is a naming convention on top of it, not a separate representation. `is_global` names the split; `combine_states` `<s\|t>` takes locals from `s` and globals from `t`. This is the splitting a procedure call needs on both ends: on entry the caller's store is saved and locals reset (`enter_state`), on exit the callee's globals are kept and the caller's locals restored (`<caller\|callee>`). Self-contained: depends only on the store, not on `com`, small-step, or the CFG. |
| definition | `is_global` | 26 | definition is_global :: "vname =&gt; bool" where<br>  "is_global x = (x = [] ∨ hd x = CHR ''G'')" | A variable is global exactly when its name is empty or starts with *G*. Treating the empty name as global makes the predicate total and keeps store entry and combination aligned. |
| definition | `combine_states` | 32 | definition combine_states :: "store =&gt; store =&gt; store"  ("&lt;_\|_&gt;" [0, 0] 1000) where<br>  "combine_states s t = (λn. if is_global n then t n else s n)" | `<s\|t>`: take locals from `s`, globals from `t`. This is the store a procedure call reconstructs on return, so the caller's locals survive the call unless the callee wrote through a global. |
| definition | `enter_state` | 41 | definition enter_state :: "store ⇒ store" where<br>  "enter_state s = (λn. if is_global n then s n else 0)" | Concrete call/scope entry: globals persist from `s`, locals reset to `0` -- the store a callee starts execution in. |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Notation.thy

> The `imp ⟦ ... ⟧` quotation produces an `VIMP_Proc.com` without HOL string quotes or qualified constructor names. The `imp` keyword distinguishes command quotation from Pure premises; whole programs use the explicit `program` prefix. Design is inspired by: https://awslabs.github.io/AutoCorrode/Unsorted/AutoCorrode/Micro_Rust_Examples.Basic_Micro_Rust.html and https://github.com/awslabs/AutoCorrode/blob/e234addc5e67f78cbff63defd24199578e8e1af3/Micro_Rust_Parsing_Frontend/Micro_Rust_Syntax.thy#L5 Inside the bracket: - bare identifiers become `V` literals (HOL string literals via @{type vname}) - numerals become `N` - arithmetic: +, -, * map to `Plus`, `Minus`, `Times` unary minus *-n* on numerals becomes `N` with a negative int - boolean comparisons: <, == map to `Less`, `Eq` - constants: true/false map to `Bc` Example: @{verbatim [display] "definition loop_prog :: VIMP_Proc.com where \"loop_prog = imp ⟦ x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 } ⟧\""}

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Proc.thy

> A call evaluates actual arguments in the caller store, binds them in a fresh callee activation, and saves the caller store with the optional destination. The runtime marker `Restore` identifies the activation boundary inside sequential syntax. An explicit `Return` publishes its value through `ret_var` and replaces pending callee commands with `Unwind`. Restoration keeps callee globals, restores caller locals, and writes the published value when the call has a destination. `Return` is the source-level early return. It publishes an optional value into `ret_var` and unwinds the current activation. `Unwind` is a runtime-only marker (like `Restore`, never in source programs): once a `Return` has fired, the computation is in `Unwind` state, discarding pending statements up to the nearest enclosing activation frame.

The runtime marker `Restore` identifies the activation boundary inside sequential syntax. An explicit `Return` publishes its value through `ret_var` and replaces pending callee commands with `Unwind`. Restoration keeps callee globals, restores caller locals, and writes the published value when the call has a destination. `Return` is the source-level early return. It publishes an optional value into `ret_var` and unwinds the current activation. `Unwind` is a runtime-only marker (like `Restore`, never in source programs): once a `Return` has fired, the computation is in `Unwind` state, discarding pending statements up to the nearest enclosing activation frame. |

| fun | `pfinal` | 168 | fun pfinal :: "com × store × frame list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "pfinal (c, s, frs) = (c = SKIP ∧ frs = [])" | Concrete Semantics calls a configuration final when no small step applies; for IMP that coincides with SKIP. (4 more lines) | Every call names a declared procedure and supplies one actual per formal. A destination requires a value-providing callee; a destination-less call may discard a published value. Procedure formals are distinct local names, and the distinguished root command contains no return. |
| fun | `no_return` | 599 | fun no_return :: "com =&gt; bool" where<br>  "no_return (Seq c1 c2) = (no_return c1 ∧ no_return c2)"<br>\| "no_return (If _ c1 c2) = (no_return c1 ∧ no_return c2)"<br>\| "no_return (While _ c) = no_return c"<br>\| "no_return (Return _) = False"<br>\| "no_return _ = True" |  |
| definition | `wf_proc_decl` | 606 | definition wf_proc_decl :: "proc_table =&gt; proc_decl =&gt; bool" where<br>  "wf_proc_decl Π decl ↔<br>     distinct (formals decl) ∧<br>     list_all valid_formal (formals decl) ∧<br>     wf_source_com Π (body decl)" |  |
| definition | `wf_source_program` | 612 | definition wf_source_program :: "proc_table =&gt; pname =&gt; com =&gt; bool" where<br>  "wf_source_program Π mnm main ↔<br>     Π mnm = Some (proc_decl_of [] main) ∧<br>     wf_source_com Π main ∧ no_return main ∧<br>     (∀p decl. Π p = Some decl ⟶ wf_proc_decl Π decl)" |  |

| fun | `string_of_aexp` | 17 | fun string_of_aexp :: "aexp ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_aexp (N n) = string_of_int n"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (V x) = x"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Plus a b) =<br>    ''('' @ string_of_aexp a @ ''+'' @ string_of_aexp b @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Minus a b) =<br>    ''('' @ string_of_aexp a @ ''-'' @ string_of_aexp b @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Times a b) =<br>    ''('' @ string_of_aexp a @ ''*'' @ string_of_aexp b @ '')''" |  |
